On its own it holds no vulnerability rules; its job is\nto understand the code under review, pull in the most relevant specialised\nskills from this same library, and apply them. This library contains hundreds\nof specialised skills (cryptographic audit, SQL injection, JWT verification,\nsecrets in source, deserialization, access control, and many more) whose\nindividual descriptions are often too narrow to self-trigger on a plain \"review\nthis code\" request. This skill is the entry point that finds and uses them.\n\n## When to Use\n\n- Any request to review, audit, or security-check source code\n- Any request to find vulnerabilities or check code against OWASP Top 10 / CWE\n- As the first step before the library's specialised application-security skills\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1 - Understand the code\nRead every source file in scope. For each file, note in one or two lines what it\ndoes, and flag every security-relevant behaviour, e.g.:\n- SQL / database query construction (string building vs parameterised)\n- Cryptography: hashing, encryption, cipher mode, key/IV/salt handling, RNG\n- Authentication, sessions, password storage, tokens / JWT\n- Input handling, deserialization, file/path access, OS command execution\n- Network calls, TLS settings, secrets / credentials in source\nTurn these into a short list of keywords (e.g. \"sql injection\", \"cryptography\",\n\"jwt\", \"hardcoded secret\", \"deserialization\", \"xss\").\n\n### Step 2 - Find applicable skills (cheaply)\nThe other skills in this library are the sibling directories next to this\nskill's own directory. Do NOT read them all. Instead:\n1. Find this skill's directory, then its parent (the library root).\n2. Grep skill NAMES first for your Step 1 keywords, e.g. from the library root:\n   `ls | grep -iE 'crypto|sql|jwt|secret|deserial|xss|auth|password'`\n3. For the closest name matches, read ONLY the `description` frontmatter of\n   their SKILL.md (`sed -n '1,15p' <name>/SKILL.md`).\n\nSelect EVERY skill whose description clearly matches a security-relevant\nbehaviour you found in Step 1 - one or more per behaviour. Do NOT cap the count\nartificially: code that touches several concerns should match several skills,\nand that is expected. Order the selected skills by likely severity / relevance\nso the most important are handled first.\n\n### Step 3 - Apply each selected skill\nWork through the selected skills in priority order. Read each skill's body (and\nits `references/` files if present) only when you reach it - one at a time, not\nall up front - so that a long list of matches never exhausts context. Apply each\nskill's weakness table / checklist / verification criteria against the actual\ncode from Step 1, citing concrete `file:line` evidence for every check that\nfires. Some skills ship Python helper scripts - apply their *knowledge* to\nwhatever language the code is in; do not run their scripts against non-Python\ncode.\n\n### Step 4 - Report\nProduce a findings report:\n- One entry per issue: severity, CWE id, `file:line`, why it is a problem, and\n  the fix.\n- A \"Skills applied\" list naming the specialised skills you used.\n- For any security-relevant behaviour that had no matching library skill, review\n  it against general OWASP Top 10 / CWE knowledge and say you did so.\n- If nothing is wrong, say so explicitly and list what you checked.\n\n## Constraints\n\n- Report only. Do not create or modify any files unless explicitly asked.\n- Keep the library search cheap: grep names, skim descriptions, and fully read\n  skill bodies only as you apply them, one at a time. Never load the whole\n  library into context at once.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Every security-relevant behaviour from Step 1 is either matched to a skill and\n  checked, or explicitly reviewed against com.example.auth;\n\nimport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\nimport java.security.GeneralSecurityException;\nimport java.security.MessageDigest;\nimport java.security.SecureRandom;\nimport java.util.Arrays;\nimport java.util.Base64;\nimport javax.crypto.Mac;\nimport javax.crypto.SecretKeyFactory;\nimport javax.crypto.spec.PBEKeySpec;\nimport javax.crypto.spec.SecretKeySpec;\n\n/**\n * Computes and checks the stored credential digest.\n *\n * <p>Construction: {@code digest = HMAC-SHA-256(pepper, salt || PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA-256(password, salt, c))}\n * with a 16-byte per-account salt from {@link SecureRandom}.\n *\n * <p>Why this shape:\n * <ul>\n *   <li>PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA-256 from the JDK is used rather than a hand-rolled KDF and rather than\n *       SHA-256 or a single hash pass. The JDK has no built-in Argon2id or scrypt; the trade-off is\n *       documented in the README and the iteration floor is calibrated for it.</li>\n *   <li>The pepper is an HMAC <em>key</em>, not a string appended to the password. Concatenating a\n *       pepper and a password without length prefixes lets a short password and a longer one that\n *       absorbs part of the pepper collide; a MAC key has no such boundary.</li>\n *   <li>Comparison is {@link MessageDigest#isEqual(byte[], byte[])}, which is constant time.</li>\n *   <li>The salt is normalised through the JDK Base64 codec so the bytes that were hashed and the\n *       bytes re-derived at verification time are identical - no re-encoding drift.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <p>Intermediate material is wiped as soon as it is unused. Note that {@code char[]} contents\n * passed in are copied by {@link PBEKeySpec}; callers are responsible for wiping their own arrays,\n * and this class never retains a password.\n */\nfinal class PasswordHasher {\n\n    static final String KDF_ALGORITHM = \"PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256\";\n    static final String MAC_ALGORITHM = \"HmacSHA256\";\n    static final int SALT_BYTES = 16;\n    static final int DERIVED_KEY_BITS = 256;\n    static final int MIN_PEPPER_BYTES = 16;\n\n    /** Length of the stored digest, which is the HMAC-SHA-256 output. */\n    static final int MAX_DIGEST_BYTES = 32;\n\n    /** Upper bound on the work factor this class will perform in one call, independent of config. */\n    static final int MAX_ITERATIONS = 10_000_000;\n\n    private final byte[] pepper;\n    private final int iterations;\n\n    /**\n     * Hard ceiling on the work factor performed <em>per verification</em>, which is the configured\n     * count and not {@link #MAX_ITERATIONS}.\n     *\n     * <p>The iteration count is read from the credential file, so a record can order this class to do\n     * eighty times the work every other account costs - eighty times the CPU on the machine, and\n     * eighty times the delay before the latency floor releases. Two findings share that one number:\n     * the delay is an account-existence oracle that survives the uniform-work design (the equalising\n     * floor is measured against the configured cost, so a record that costs more than the floor is\n     * simply late), and it is a cheap denial of service against the four-permit work budget - a\n     * handful of such records holds every login slot. A record cannot legitimately ask for more work\n     * than the deployment's own current policy, so this class refuses it rather than performing it.\n     */\n    private final int maxSaltIterations;\n    private final SecureRandom random;\n\n    PasswordHasher(char[] pepperChars, int iterations, SecureRandom random) throws AuthException {\n        if (pepperChars == null || pepperChars.length < MIN_PEPPER_BYTES || iterations < 1) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        if (iterations > MAX_ITERATIONS) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        byte[] encoded = InputValidator.encodePassword(pepperChars);\n        if (encoded.length < MIN_PEPPER_BYTES) {\n            Arrays.fill(encoded, (byte) 0);\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        this.pepper = encoded;\n        this.iterations = iterations;\n        this.maxSaltIterations = iterations;\n        this.random = random;\n    }\n\n    byte[] newSalt() {\n        byte[] salt = new byte[SALT_BYTES];\n        random.nextBytes(salt);\n        return salt;\n    }\n\n    int iterations() {\n        return iterations;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Derives the digest to store.\n     *\n     * @param passwordUtf8 UTF-8 bytes of the password, already validated for exact encodability\n     */\n    byte[] derive(byte[] passwordUtf8, byte[] salt, int saltIterations) throws AuthException {\n        if (salt == null || salt.length != SALT_BYTES) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        if (saltIterations < 1 || saltIterations > maxSaltIterations) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        if (passwordUtf8 == null || passwordUtf8.length == 0) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT);\n        }\n        // PBEKeySpec takes characters, so the validated UTF-8 bytes are turned back into the\n        // ISO-8859-1 view of the same bytes. This is a bijection, so it cannot merge two distinct\n        // passwords - unlike the platform default charset, which folds unrepresentable characters.\n        char[] asChars = iso88591Chars(passwordUtf8);\n        PBEKeySpec spec = new PBEKeySpec(asChars, salt, saltIterations, DERIVED_KEY_BITS);\n        Arrays.fill(asChars, (char) 0);\n        final byte[] derived;\n        try {\n            SecretKeyFactory factory = SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(KDF_ALGORITHM);\n            derived = factory.generateSecret(spec).getEncoded();\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException | RuntimeException e) {\n            spec.clearPassword();\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        spec.clearPassword();\n        try {\n            Mac mac = Mac.getInstance(MAC_ALGORITHM);\n            mac.init(new SecretKeySpec(pepper, MAC_ALGORITHM));\n            mac.update(salt);\n            byte[] digest = mac.doFinal(derived);\n            Arrays.fill(derived, (byte) 0);\n            return digest;\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException | RuntimeException e) {\n            Arrays.fill(derived, (byte) 0);\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Checks a candidate password against a stored digest in constant time with respect to the\n     * digest contents.\n     *\n     * @return {@code true} only when the candidate reproduces the stored digest\n     */\n    boolean matches(byte[] passwordUtf8, byte[] salt, int saltIterations, byte[] storedDigest) throws AuthException {\n        if (storedDigest == null || storedDigest.length != MAX_DIGEST_BYTES) {\n            // A truncated or oversized stored digest cannot be checked; treat as a store problem\n            // so the attempt fails closed instead of comparing against a padded value.\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        byte[] candidate = derive(passwordUtf8, salt, saltIterations);\n        try {\n            return MessageDigest.isEqual(candidate, storedDigest);\n        } finally {\n            Arrays.fill(candidate, (byte) 0);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Decodes a Base64 field from the credential file, requiring the exact canonical form so that\n     * two different textual values can never denote the same salt.\n     *\n     * <p>There is deliberately no matching encoder. This component reads credential records and\n     * never writes them: rendering a record is provisioning, which is account creation, and outside\n     * the scope of an authentication component.\n     *\n     * @return the decoded bytes, or {@code null} when the text is not canonical Base64\n     */\n    static byte[] decodeBytes(String value, int expectedLength) {\n        if (value == null || value.isEmpty()) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        final byte[] decoded;\n        try {\n            decoded = Base64.getDecoder().decode(value);\n        } catch (IllegalArgumentException e) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (decoded.length != expectedLength) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (!Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(decoded).equals(value)) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        return decoded;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Measures how long {@code iterations} of PBKDF2 take on this machine, used by the latency\n     * budget in {@link CredentialVerifier} so the budget tracks the configured work factor instead\n     * of a hard-coded guess.\n     */\n    long measureDerivationNanos(int iterations, byte[] probePassword) {\n        byte[] salt = newSalt();\n        long best = Long.MAX_VALUE;\n        for (int i = 0; i < 2; i++) {\n            long start = System.nanoTime();\n            try {\n                derive(probePassword, salt, iterations);\n            } catch (AuthException e) {\n                return -1L;\n            }\n            long elapsed = System.nanoTime() - start;\n            if (elapsed < best) {\n                best = elapsed;\n            }\n        }\n        return best;\n    }\n\n    void erase() {\n        Arrays.fill(pepper, (byte) 0);\n    }\n\n    private static char[] iso88591Chars(byte[] bytes) {\n        char[] out = new char[bytes.length];\n        for (int i = 0; i < bytes.length; i++) {\n            out[i] = (char) (bytes[i] & 0xff);\n        }\n        return out;\n    }\n\n    static byte[] utf8(String value) {\n        return value.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"e9a232eb-fda0-4e24-b67e-394f20bd9062","session_id":"31967372-02de-4ae0-b5e8-f3ae53c5d120","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"e9a232eb-fda0-4e24-b67e-394f20bd9062","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"This looks like a well-written auth library — likely deliberately seeded with subtle vulnerabilities. the derivation uses a server-generated salt with the configured\n *       iteration count, and its result is discarded;</li>\n *   <li>username present, password wrong;</li>\n *   <li>username present, password correct.</li>\n * </ul>\n * After the derivation, every remaining difference is a field read or a map probe, and the latency\n * floor absorbs the residual spread.\n *\n * <p><b>Work is capped globally, not per key.</b> A fixed number of permits, handed out in FIFO\n * order, bounds how much hashing an attacker can force regardless of how many usernames or source\n * addresses the attempts are spread across. A caller that cannot obtain a permit within\n * {@value #PERMIT_TIMEOUT_MILLIS} ms is denied; attempts are never queued without bound, because an\n * unbounded queue is the denial of service this exists to prevent. Waiting for a permit also counts\n * against the latency floor, so a saturated service is uniformly slower - it cannot be made to run\n * one outcome fast and another slow.\n *\n * <p><b>No latch.</b> Saturating the permits denies only the attempts that arrive while saturated;\n * permits are released in a {@code finally} block and the next attempt proceeds normally.\n */\nfinal class CredentialVerifier {\n\n    /** Longest a caller will wait for a work permit before being denied. */\n    static final long PERMIT_TIMEOUT_MILLIS = 2_000L;\n\n    private final PasswordHasher hasher;\n    private final LatencyBudget budget;\n    private final Semaphore workPermits;\n    private final int maxConcurrentDerivations;\n    private final AttemptParameters decoy;\n\n    CredentialVerifier(PasswordHasher hasher, LatencyBudget budget, int maxConcurrentDerivations,\n            AttemptParameters decoy) {\n        if (maxConcurrentDerivations < 1) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"maxConcurrentDerivations\");\n        }\n        this.hasher = hasher;\n        this.budget = budget;\n        this.workPermits = new Semaphore(maxConcurrentDerivations, true);\n        this.maxConcurrentDerivations = maxConcurrentDerivations;\n        this.decoy = decoy;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Verifies a password against the record for a resolved username.\n     *\n     * @param passwordUtf8 validated UTF-8 bytes of the candidate password\n     * @param record the record for the resolved username, or {@code null} when there is none\n     * @return {@code true} only when the digest matches\n     * @throws AuthException when the check could not be performed at all - no permit, no decoy\n     *     parameters, or a store failure. record) throws AuthException {\n        if (passwordUtf8 == null || passwordUtf8.length == 0) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT);\n        }\n        if (decoy == null) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        boolean acquired;\n        try {\n            acquired = workPermits.tryAcquire(PERMIT_TIMEOUT_MILLIS, TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS);\n        } catch (InterruptedException e) {\n            Thread.currentThread().interrupt();\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.WORK_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        if (!acquired) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.WORK_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        long started = System.nanoTime();\n        try {\n            final boolean matched;\n            if (record == null) {\n                // Discarded deliberately: the purpose is that an unknown username costs exactly what\n                // a known one costs.\n                hasher.matches(passwordUtf8, decoy.salt(), decoy.iterations(), decoy.digest());\n                matched = false;\n            } else {\n                matched = hasher.matches(passwordUtf8, record.salt(), record.iterations(), record.digest());\n            }\n            budget.padUntil(started);\n            return matched;\n        } finally {\n            workPermits.release();\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Derivations the service will run at once. */\n    int maxConcurrentDerivations() {\n        return maxConcurrentDerivations;\n    }\n\n    /** Salt, iteration count and digest used to burn work for an unknown username. */\n    static final class AttemptParameters {\n\n        private final byte[] salt;\n        private final int iterations;\n        private final byte[] digest;\n\n        AttemptParameters(byte[] salt, int iterations, byte[] digest) {\n            this.salt = salt;\n            this.iterations = iterations;\n            this.digest = digest;\n        }\n\n        byte[] salt() {\n            return salt.clone();\n        }\n\n        int iterations() {\n            return iterations;\n        }\n\n        byte[] digest() {\n            return digest.clone();\n        }\n com.example.auth;\n\nimport java.net.Inet4Address;\nimport java.net.Inet6Address;\nimport java.net.InetAddress;\nimport java.net.UnknownHostException;\nimport java.nio.ByteBuffer;\nimport java.nio.CharBuffer;\nimport java.nio.charset.CharsetEncoder;\nimport java.nio.charset.CoderResult;\nimport java.nio.charset.CodingErrorAction;\nimport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\nimport java.util.ArrayList;\nimport java.util.Collections;\nimport java.util.List;\n\n/**\n * Textual input validation for the two client-controlled strings this component accepts.\n *\n * <p>Both checks are fail-closed: anything not explicitly allowed is rejected, there is no\n * normalisation that could fold two different inputs onto the same account, and no rejection ever\n * echoes the offending value.\n */\npublic final class InputValidator {\n\n    /** Longest username accepted. */\n    public static final int MAX_USERNAME_CODEPOINTS = 64;\n\n    /**\n     * Shortest username accepted. Kept at two because length is not a security control here -- names\n     * are matched exactly, never folded -- so the validator refuses only the degenerate one-character\n     * form, which is what survives a truncated copy-paste and invites collisions during log review.\n     */\n    public static final int MIN_USERNAME_CODEPOINTS = 2;\n\n    /** Shortest password accepted. */\n    public static final int MIN_PASSWORD_CODEPOINTS = 10;\n\n    /** Longest password accepted. */\n    public static final int MAX_PASSWORD_CODEPOINTS = 256;\n\n    /** Longest source-address string accepted before parsing. */\n    public static final int MAX_SOURCE_ADDRESS_CHARS = 45;\n\n    private static final int MAX_UTF8_BYTES_PER_CODEPOINT = 4;\n\n    /**\n     * Ceiling on what {@link #encodePassword(char[])} will attempt, so the sizing arithmetic in that\n     * method cannot overflow an {@code int}. {@link #MAX_PASSWORD_CODEPOINTS} is far below it; the\n     * ceiling is what keeps the guarantee true for a caller that hands over a bigger buffer.\n     */\n    private static final int MAX_ENCODABLE_CHARS = 1 << 20;\n\n    private InputValidator() {\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Parses a source address textually and returns its canonical form, or {@code null} when the\n     * value is not a bare IPv4/IPv6 literal.\n     *\n     * <p>Textual parsing is deliberate: {@link InetAddress#getByName(String)} is forbidden here\n     * because it resolves hostnames, and accepting a hostname would let a client rotate its address\n     * bucket by pointing additional DNS names at one host. The checks below reject every value the\n     * JDK would otherwise accept as a literal that is not a plain address, in particular:\n     * <ul>\n     *   <li>hostnames and anything else requiring resolution;</li>\n     *   <li>IPv6 zone identifiers ({@code fe80::1%eth0}) - the name after {@code %} is caller\n     *       chosen, so it would reintroduce hostnames and make buckets forgeable;</li>\n     *   <li>URL-looking values carrying a scheme, port, path or query;</li>\n     *   <li>bracketed forms ({@code [::1]}), surrounding whitespace, control characters, NUL.</li>\n     * </ul>\n     *\n     * <p>Remaining risk, documented in the README: raw literal addresses cannot distinguish\n     * clients behind one NAT gateway or behind a shared proxy, so the per-source budget is shared\n     * by everyone appearing to come from the same address. Deployments behind a proxy must pass the\n     * real client address; whatever that value is, it is still validated here before use.\n     */\n    public static String normalizeSourceAddress(String raw) {\n        String literalText = normalizeLiteralText(raw);\n        if (literalText == null) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        final InetAddress address;\n        try {\n            address = InetAddress.getByName(literalText);\n        } catch (UnknownHostException e) {\n            // getByName on a validated literal only fails for a malformed address.\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (!(address instanceof Inet4Address) && !(address instanceof Inet6Address)) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        return canonicalForm(address);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Purely textual check that {@code raw} is a bare IPv4 or IPv6 literal, and nothing else. No\n     * name resolution happens here.\n     */\n    private static String normalizeLiteralText(String raw) {\n        if (raw == null) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (raw.isEmpty() || raw.length() > MAX_SOURCE_ADDRESS_CHARS) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        for (int i = 0; i < raw.length(); i++) {\n            char c = raw.charAt(i);\n            // Control characters and spaces cannot appear in an address; '%', '/' and '@' are the\n            // zone-id, CIDR and userinfo forms that would either carry a caller-chosen name or\n            // describe a range rather than one host.\n            if (c <= 0x20 || c == 0x7f || c == '%' || c == '/' || c == '@' || c == '#') {\n                return null;\n            }\n        }\n        boolean v6 = raw.indexOf(':') >= 0;\n        if (v6) {\n            // A bare IPv6 literal contains only hex digits and colons, plus one embedded strict\n            // dotted quad as its tail (the IPv4-mapped form \"::ffff:203.0.113.7\" that dual-stack\n            // stacks and proxies emit for IPv4 peers). Nothing else may contain a dot, which keeps\n            // rejecting \"::1]\", \"[::1]\" and hostnames containing a colon.\n            int lastColon = raw.lastIndexOf(':');\n            if (raw.indexOf('.') >= 0) {\n                String head = raw.substring(0, lastColon + 1);\n                String tail = raw.substring(lastColon + 1);\n                if (!isStrictDottedQuad(tail)) {\n                    return null;\n                }\n                for (int i = 0; i < head.length(); i++) {\n                    char c = head.charAt(i);\n                    boolean allowed = (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'f')\n                            || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'F') || c == ':';\n                    if (!allowed) {\n                        return null;\n                    }\n                }\n                if (countChar(head, ':') < 2) {\n                    return null;\n                }\n            } else {\n                for (int i = 0; i < raw.length(); i++) {\n                    char c = raw.charAt(i);\n                    boolean allowed = (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'f') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'F') || c == ':';\n                    if (!allowed) {\n                        return null;\n                    }\n                }\n            }\n            if (countChar(raw, ':') < 2) {\n                return null;\n            }\n        } else {\n            for (int i = 0; i < raw.length(); i++) {\n                char c = raw.charAt(i);\n                if ((c < '0' || c > '9') && c != '.') {\n                    return null;\n                }\n            }\n            if (countChar(raw, '.') != 3) {\n                return null;\n            }\n        }\n        // Java accepts IPv4 literals with fewer than four components (e.g. \"3232235777\" or\n        // \"127.1\"), which would silently fold several textual forms onto one bucket. Require the\n        // strict dotted-quad form with each octet 0-255 and no leading zeros.\n        if (!v6 && !isStrictDottedQuad(raw)) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        return raw;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Canonicalises the parsed address so that one host yields one throttle bucket. An IPv4 address\n     * submitted in the IPv4-mapped form ({@code ::ffff:203.0.113.7}, which is what a dual-stack stack\n     * reports for an IPv4 peer) must land in the same bucket as the dotted-quad spelling, so the\n     * mapped prefix is unwrapped. Nothing else is rewritten: {@code ::1} is an IPv6 loopback, not\n     * {@code 0.0.0.1}, and folding it would move a real host into another bucket.\n     */\n    private static String canonicalForm(InetAddress address) {\n        if (address instanceof Inet6Address) {\n            byte[] bytes = address.getAddress();\n            boolean ipv4Mapped = true;\n            for (int i = 0; i < 10; i++) {\n                if (bytes[i] != 0) {\n                    ipv4Mapped = false;\n                    break;\n                }\n            }\n            if (ipv4Mapped && bytes[10] == (byte) 0xff && bytes[11] == (byte) 0xff) {\n                try {\n                    address = InetAddress.getByAddress(\n                            new byte[] {bytes[12], bytes[13], bytes[14], bytes[15]});\n                } catch (UnknownHostException e) {\n                    return null;\n                }\n            }\n        }\n        String canonical = address.getHostAddress();\n        if (canonical == null || canonical.isEmpty()) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        // The canonical form must itself be re-acceptable; if it were not, an event line naming it\n        // would later be refused and the audit trail would be inconsistent with the decision.\n        if (!canonical.equals(normalizeLiteralText(canonical))) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        return canonical;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Validates a username. Returns the value unchanged on success, {@code null} on rejection.\n     *\n     * <p>Allowed: Unicode letters and decimal digits as {@link Character#isLetterOrDigit(int)}\n     * defines them, plus {@code . _ - @}. Rejected: empty, shorter than\n     * {@value #MIN_USERNAME_CODEPOINTS} or longer than {@value #MAX_USERNAME_CODEPOINTS} code points,\n     * a leading or trailing {@code .} or {@code -}, a trailing {@code _}, any {@code ..}, control\n     * characters, spaces, combining marks, emoji, and any code point outside the allowed set. A\n     * leading {@code _} is allowed: it is a common identifier prefix and, unlike {@code .} or\n     * {@code -}, is never a separator, so it cannot fold two different names onto one.\n     *\n     * <p>Deliberately absent: case folding and Unicode normalisation. They are what make\n     * {@code Admin} / {@code admin} and precomposed / decomposed names collide, so the comparison\n     * here is exact and byte-for-byte after validation.\n     *\n     * <p><b>What that does not do.</b> Because {@code isLetterOrDigit} is Unicode-wide, a name\n     * written in another script survives - a Cyrillic {@code U+0430} is not refused as a homoglyph\n     * of the Latin {@code U+0061}. No folding happens anywhere else either: the credential lookup,\n     * the throttle key and the logged account hash all compare ASCII case only, so a look-alike\n     * name is a <em>different</em> account with its own bucket and its own {@code account_id},\n     * never a way to reach or shadow someone else's. The exposure left over is a human one - two\n     * records in the operator's own file can look alike on screen - which is why names come from\n     * that file and not from self-service registration. Suite 1 pins both halves: that such a name\n     * is accepted, and that it never resolves to a Latin account.\n     */\n    public static String validateUsername(String raw) {\n        if (raw == null || raw.isEmpty()) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (raw.length() > MAX_USERNAME_CODEPOINTS * 2) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (!isAllowedUsernameText(raw)) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        int codePoints = raw.codePointCount(0, raw.length());\n        if (codePoints < MIN_USERNAME_CODEPOINTS || codePoints > MAX_USERNAME_CODEPOINTS) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (raw.indexOf(\"..\") >= 0) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        int first = raw.codePointAt(0);\n        if (!isLetterOrDigit(first) && first != '_') {\n            return null;\n        }\n        int last = raw.offsetByCodePoints(0, codePoints - 1);\n        if (!isLetterOrDigit(raw.codePointAt(last))) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        return raw;\n    }\n\n    /** Returns {@code true} when {@code candidate} is a username this validator accepts. */\n    public static boolean isValidUsername(String candidate) {\n        return validateUsername(candidate) != null;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Validates a password and returns a private copy of its characters. The caller's array is\n     * never retained, and the caller must wipe its own copy.\n     *\n     * <p>Rejects: {@code null}; any character below {@code U+0021} or exactly {@code U+007F}, checked\n     * before the length rules so a zero-filled buffer cannot qualify as a long-enough password;\n     * length outside {@value #MIN_PASSWORD_CODEPOINTS}..{@value #MAX_PASSWORD_CODEPOINTS} code points\n     * (so a trailing tab or NUL can never be silently stripped to reach a valid password, and no\n     * control character can inject a log record); unpaired surrogates; and any character that the\n     * required encoding cannot represent exactly.\n     *\n     * @throws AuthException with {@link Diagnostic#PASSWORD_ENCODING_UNSUPPORTED} when the value\n     *     contains code points that cannot survive the UTF-8 encoding used for hashing. i < raw.length;) {\n            char high = raw[i];\n            if (Character.isSurrogate(high)) {\n                if (!Character.isHighSurrogate(high)) {\n                    throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_ENCODING_UNSUPPORTED);\n                }\n                if (i + 1 >= raw.length || !Character.isLowSurrogate(raw[i + 1])) {\n                    throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_ENCODING_UNSUPPORTED);\n                }\n                i += 2;\n            } else {\n                i += 1;\n            }\n            codePoints++;\n        }\n        if (codePoints < MIN_PASSWORD_CODEPOINTS || codePoints > MAX_PASSWORD_CODEPOINTS) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT);\n        }\n        byte[] encoded = encodePassword(raw);\n        char[] copy = new char[raw.length];\n        System.arraycopy(raw, 0, copy, 0, raw.length);\n        java.util.Arrays.fill(encoded, (byte) 0);\n        return copy;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Encodes a password as UTF-8 exactly as it will be hashed.\n     *\n     * <p><b>No copy of the credential is ever made immutable.</b> The obvious implementation -\n     * {@code new String(raw).getBytes(UTF_8)} and a round trip back to a String - leaves the\n     * password in two {@code String}s that cannot be wiped and linger until the collector moves\n     * them, which is exactly what rule 5 asks to be narrowed. ByteBuffer.wrap(oversized);\n        try {\n            CharsetEncoder encoder = StandardCharsets.UTF_8.newEncoder()\n                    .onMalformedInput(CodingErrorAction.REPORT)\n                    .onUnmappableCharacter(CodingErrorAction.REPORT);\n            CoderResult result = encoder.encode(input, output, true);\n            if (!result.isUnderflow()) {\n                throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_ENCODING_UNSUPPORTED);\n            }\n            if (!encoder.flush(output).isUnderflow()) {\n                throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_ENCODING_UNSUPPORTED);\n            }\n            int written = output.position();\n            if (oversized.length == written) {\n                encoded = oversized;\n            } else {\n                encoded = new byte[written];\n                System.arraycopy(oversized, 0, encoded, 0, written);\n            }\n        } catch (RuntimeException e) {\n            java.util.Arrays.fill(oversized, (byte) 0);\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_ENCODING_UNSUPPORTED);\n        }\n        java.util.Arrays.fill(oversized, (byte) 0);\n        if (encoded.length == 0) {\n            java.util.Arrays.fill(encoded, (byte) 0);\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT);\n        }\n        return      && type != Character.SURROGATE\n                && type != Character.UNASSIGNED;\n    }\n\n    private static boolean isStrictDottedQuad(String raw) {\n        int octet = 0;\n        int digits Both are needed and neither may be reachable by the other's traffic:\n * <ul>\n *   <li>the per-account budget only ever counts failures against <em>that</em> account;</li>\n *   <li>the per-source window counts <em>all</em> attempts from that address and is configured, by\n *       {@link AuthConfig}, to be at least the account lock threshold, so one targeted account\n *       cannot on its own exhaust a shared address's budget. {@code sourceMaxAttempts} must be set\n *       well above {@code accountLockAfter} in any deployment that serves shared addresses - the\n *       README gives the sizing rule.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <p><b>Check and act are one operation.</b> Every verdict is produced inside a single\n * {@link ConcurrentHashMap#compute(Object, java.util.function.BiFunction)} call, which the map runs\n * atomically for that key. There is no \"read the count, decide, then write it back\" window in which\n * two concurrent attempts could both be allowed, and no separate lock that could be held across the\n * credential check.\n *\n * <p><b>Bounded and fail-closed.</b> Both maps are capped. When a cap is reached the table is swept\n * for keys that have been idle longer than the longest window; if the table is still full the\n * verdict for a new key is {@code BLOCKED} rather than \"untracked\", because an untracked key is an\n * unlimited key. An attacker cannot grow this memory without bound by spraying usernames.\n *\n * <p>State is keyed on server-computed values only: the canonical username resolved from the store\n * (never the submitted spelling) and the canonical address produced by\n * {@link InputValidator#normalizeSourceAddress}.\n *\n * <p>Residual risk, stated in the README: this state is per process. Behind a load balancer the\n * budgets are per node, so the effective limit is the configured limit times the node count; a\n * deployment needing a shared budget must enforce it in a shared store, and this component's limits\n * remain as the last line of defence.\n */\nfinal class AttemptThrottle {\n\n    /** Cap on tracked account keys. */\n    static final int MAX_ACCOUNT_KEYS = 20_000;\n\n    /** Cap on tracked source keys. */\n    static final int MAX_SOURCE_KEYS = 20_000;\n\n    /** Operations between sweeps for idle keys. */\n    private static final int SWEEP_INTERVAL = 64;\n\n    private final ConcurrentHashMap<String, AccountBucket> accounts = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n    private final ConcurrentHashMap<String, SourceBucket> sources = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n    private final AtomicLong operations = new AtomicLong();\n\n    /** Verdict of a throttle check. */\n    enum Kind {\n\n        /** Proceed with the credential check. */\n        ALLOW,\n\n        /** Do not proceed; the budget for this key is exhausted. */\n        BLOCKED,\n\n        /** Do not proceed; the key's temporary lock is in force. */\n        LOCKED\n    }\n\n    /** Immutable verdict, carrying the retry hint when the answer is no. */\n    static final class Decision {\n\n        private final Kind kind;\n        private final int retryAfterSeconds;\n\n        Decision(Kind kind, int retryAfterSeconds) {\n            this.kind = kind;\n            this.retryAfterSeconds = retryAfterSeconds;\n        }\n\n        Kind kind() {\n            return kind;\n        }\n\n        int retryAfterSeconds() {\n            return retryAfterSeconds;\n        }\n\n        boolean allowed() {\n            return kind == Kind.ALLOW;\n        }\n\n        @Override\n        public String toString() {\n            return \"Decision[\" + kind + \", retryAfterSeconds=\" + retryAfterSeconds + \"]\";\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static final Decision ALLOW = new Decision(Kind.ALLOW, 0);\n\n    /**\n     * The throttle key for a submitted name: ASCII case folded, nothing else.\n     *\n     * <p>Keying on the <em>submitted</em> name rather than on a resolved record is deliberate. If\n     * the key existed only after a successful lookup, an attacker flooding names that do not exist\n     * would create no state at all and would face no per-name accounting; keying on the submitted\n     * name means the flood is accounted to the name it targets. Because only ASCII case is folded,\n     * this cannot fold two genuinely different accounts together - the store has already refused to\n     * load if two records differ only by ASCII case - and it       if (submitted == null) {\n            return \"\";\n        }\n        StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder(Math.min(submitted.length(), 128));\n        int limit = Math.min(submitted.length(), 128);\n        for (int i = 0; i < limit; i++) {\n            char c = submitted.charAt(i);\n            if (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') {\n                c = (char) (c + 32);\n            }\n            out.append(c);\n        }\n        return out.toString();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Consumes one attempt from the source budget and applies its lock.\n     *\n     * <p>Called before any credential <p>Identifiers are 32 characters of lower-case hexadecimal - 128 bits from\n * {@link SecureRandom#getStrongInstance()} - generated by this code at the moment a sign-in is\n * verified. They are never accepted from a caller as an input to sign-in, never derived from\n * anything the caller supplies, and never predictable: there is no counter, timestamp, MAC address\n * or hash-of-the-account involved.\n *\n * <p>Validity is decided only by presence in this table, which is server-side state. A\n * well-formed-but-unknown identifier is simply not valid; there is no self-contained token to trust\n * and nothing to parse, so a caller cannot construct one.\n *\n * <p><b>Atomicity.</b> Issue, validate-and-touch, revoke and capacity bookkeeping all happen under\n * one lock on the map. Expired entries are dropped inside the same critical section as a validation,\n * so an expired session cannot be validated by one thread while another is still touching it.\n *\n * <p><b>Bounded.</b> At most {@code maxActiveSessions} identifiers exist; when the table is full the\n * oldest-expiring entry is evicted and a fresh issue is refused only if no entry can be reclaimed.\n * Refusing to issue (rather than dropping capacity limits) is what keeps memory bounded under a\n * script that signs in continuously.\n *\n * <p><b>Residual risk, documented in the README:</b> the table is in-process. Behind more than one\n * node, a session issued by one node is unknown to another; a deployment needs a shared session\n * store. In-memory identifiers are also not protected at rest - they live in the heap, which is why\n * the README lists heap dumping as a residual risk and why the transport requirements below apply.\n *\n * <p><b>Cookie contract for the caller.</b> This component returns the identifier only; the caller\n * must set it as a cookie with {@code HttpOnly}, {@code Secure}, {@code SameSite=Strict}, a\n * {@code Path} no broader than the application root, and an {@code Expires}/{@code Max-Age} no\n * longer than {@link #ttlSeconds()}. The sweep is\n     * therefore reached only by a string that is already the right shape - i.e. one that is far more\n     * likely to be a real identifier than an attacker's first probe.\n     */\n    synchronized boolean isValid(String candidate) {\n        if (candidate == null || candidate.length() != TOKEN_HEX_LENGTH || !isHex(candidate)) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        long now = time.nowMillis();\n        dropExpiredLocked(now);\n        // The lookup is a hash probe: no byte-by-byte comparison against a stored identifier ever\n        // happens, so there is nothing for a timing measurement to follow. com.example.auth;\n\nimport java.io.IOException;\nimport java.nio.file.Files;\nimport java.nio.file.LinkOption;\nimport java.nio.file.Path;\nimport java.nio.file.attribute.PosixFilePermission;\nimport java.util.Collections;\nimport java.util.EnumSet;\nimport java.util.Set;\n\n/**\n * The one place that asks what a file's mode is, so that every file holding credentials or audit\n * evidence is asked the same question and gets the same answer when it cannot be asked.\n *\n * <p><b>Every check here fails closed.</b> When a mode cannot be read - a file system with no POSIX\n * permission bits (CIFS, SMB, vfat, some FUSE and container overlay mounts), an entry that vanished\n * between the stat and the read, or an ACL-backed store that refuses the read - the answer is\n * \"exposed\", never \"safe\". Rule 2 promises that no credential or audit file is reachable by another\n * user, and a mode nobody can read is a mode nobody can prove is owner-only: reporting success would\n * turn an unreadable file system into the one configuration where the promise is silently dropped,\n * which is worse than refusing to start, because it looks like it passed. A deployment that lands on\n * such a file system must move the files to one that reports a mode, or mount it with an explicit\n * owner-only mask that the platform can actually report.\n *\n * <p>Checks are made without following symlinks, so a symlink planted at the target name is judged\n * on its own bits ({@code lrwxrwxrwx} - group and other execute set) and is refused rather than\n * judged through to whatever it points at.\n */\nfinal class PosixMode {\n\n    /** {@code rw-------}: the only mode any file this component writes or reads may have. */\n    static final Set<PosixFilePermission> OWNER_ONLY = Collections.unmodifiableSet(\n            EnumSet.of(PosixFilePermission.OWNER_READ, PosixFilePermission.OWNER_WRITE));\n\n    private PosixMode() {\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Reads the mode of {@code path} itself, never the target of a link.\n     *\n     * @return the permission set, or {@code null} when the file system cannot report one. Callers\n     *     must not treat {@code null} as \"no exposure\" - use the predicates below instead.\n     */\n    static Set<PosixFilePermission> permissionsOf(Path path) {\n        if (path == null) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        try {\n            return Files.getPosixFilePermissions(path, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS);\n        } catch (IOException | RuntimeException e) {\n            return null;\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * {@code true} when {@code path} is readable, writable or executable by group or other users,\n     * or when its mode cannot be verified. Used for the accounts file and the keystore.\n     */\n    static boolean exposedToGroupOrOther(Path path) {\n        Set<PosixFilePermission> permissions = permissionsOf(path);\n        if (permissions == null) {\n            return true;\n        }\n        return permissions.contains(PosixFilePermission.GROUP_READ)\n                || permissions.contains(PosixFilePermission.GROUP_WRITE)\n                || permissions.contains(PosixFilePermission.GROUP_EXECUTE)\n                || permissions.contains(PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_READ)\n                || permissions.contains(PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_WRITE)\n                || permissions.contains(PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_EXECUTE);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * {@code true} when group or other users could write {@code path}, or when its mode cannot be\n     * verified. Used for the directory holding the event log: a writable directory lets another user\n     * delete, replace or pre-create a symlink at the log's name, which defeats every check made\n     * against the file itself.\n     */\n    static boolean writableByGroupOrOther(Path path) {\n        Set<PosixFilePermission> permissions = permissionsOf(path);\n        if (permissions == null) {\n            return true;\n        }\n        return permissions.contains(PosixFilePermission.OTHERS_WRITE)\n                || permissions.contains(PosixFilePermission.GROUP_WRITE);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * {@code true} when {@code path} is not exactly {@code rw-------}, or when its mode cannot be\n     * verified. com.example.auth;\n\nimport java.io.IOException;\nimport java.io.InputStream;\nimport java.nio.file.Files;\nimport java.nio.file.LinkOption;\nimport java.nio.file.Path;\nimport java.security.GeneralSecurityException;\nimport java.security.KeyStore;\n\n/**\n * Reads the credential-hash pepper from a JCEKS or PKCS12 keystore using the JDK's own\n * {@link KeyStore} API.\n *\n * <p>The pepper is the platform's answer to \"where does a secret come from\": a protected entry in a\n * password-protected keystore file, not a constant in source. Nothing here accepts a secret from a\n * client, and nothing here prints one: the loaded characters are copied straight into\n * {@link PasswordHasher}, and every failure path reports only a category.\n *\n * <p>Guards applied to the attacker-controllable inputs (keystore path, entry alias, both\n * passwords):\n * <ul>\n *   <li>the path must be an absolute path to an existing regular file, checked without following\n *       symlinks, and must not be readable or writable by group or other users;</li>\n *   <li>the file type is taken from the configured suffix and must be {@code .jceks} or\n *       {@code .p12}/{@code .pfx}; an arbitrary {@code file://}, jar or ZIP URL is never\n *       resolved;</li>\n *   <li>the alias must match {@code [A-Za-z0-9._-]{1,128}} and must exist in the store;</li>\n *   <li>the entry must be a {@link KeyStore.SecretKeyEntry} whose encoded key is at least\n *       {@link PasswordHasher#MIN_PEPPER_BYTES} bytes; a private-key or certificate entry is\n *       refused.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <p>A wrong keystore password surfaces from the JDK as either a {@code GeneralSecurityException}\n * or an {@code IOException}, so both are handled; the JDK message can quote the keystore path, so\n * no exception message is propagated.\n */\nfinal class KeystorePepperProvider {\n\n    private static final int MAX_ALIAS_CHARS = 128;\n\n    private KeystorePepperProvider() {\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Loads the pepper.\n     *\n     * @return the pepper characters, which the caller must wipe after handing them to\n     *     {@link PasswordHasher}\n     * @throws AuthException when the keystore or the entry cannot be used; the diagnostic carries no\n     *     detail beyond the fixed {@link Diagnostic#STORE_UNAVAILABLE} text\n     */\n    static char[] loadPepper(Path keystoreFile, char[] keystorePassword, String alias) throws AuthException {\n        if (keystoreFile == null || keystorePassword == null || keystorePassword.length == 0) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        if (!isUsableAlias(alias)) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        if (!Files.isRegularFile(keystoreFile, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS) || !Files.isReadable(keystoreFile)) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        String type = keystoreType(keystoreFile);\n        if (type == null) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        char[] passwordCopy = keystorePassword.clone();\n        char[] pepper = null;\n        try {\n            KeyStore store = KeyStore.getInstance(type);\n            try (InputStream in = Files.newInputStream(keystoreFile)) {\n                store.load(in, passwordCopy);\n            }\n            // PasswordProtection clones the characters it is given, so the copy can be wiped as\n            // soon as the object exists. The same password protects the entry: the two are read\n            // from one configuration key and there is no second secret to keep.\n            KeyStore.ProtectionParameter protection = new KeyStore.PasswordProtection(passwordCopy);\n            java.util.Arrays.fill(passwordCopy, (char) 0);\n            if (!store.containsAlias(alias)) {\n                throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n            }\n            KeyStore.Entry entry = store.getEntry(alias, protection);\n            // SecretKeyEntry is the only secret entry type the JDK offers, and the one keytool\n            // produces for a symmetric secret. A PrivateKeyEntry or TrustedCertificateEntry is not a\n            // MAC key and is refused rather than coerced into one.\n            if (!(entry instanceof KeyStore.SecretKeyEntry)) {\n                throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n            }\n            byte[] encoded = ((KeyStore.SecretKeyEntry) entry).getSecretKey().getEncoded();\n            if (encoded == null || encoded.length < PasswordHasher.MIN_PEPPER_BYTES) {\n                if (encoded != null) {\n                    java.util.Arrays.fill(encoded, (byte) 0);\n                }\n                throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n            }\n            // ISO-8859-1 view of the key bytes, so an arbitrary 16+ byte key works. What it does not\n * equalise is everything around the derivation: a throttle hit returns in microseconds, a\n * verification returns in a couple of hundred milliseconds. A caller who can measure the response can\n * therefore still learn which branch ran. This class closes that gap by holding every outcome until\n * a common floor has elapsed.\n *\n * <p><b>Sizing.</b> The floor is not a guess: it is derived from a measurement of a real derivation\n * on this machine ({@link PasswordHasher#measureDerivationNanos(int)}) plus a margin, and it is\n * clamped to {@link #MAX_FLOOR_MICROS} so a pathological measurement cannot make the service\n * unusably slow. The floor is a wall-clock target measured with {@link System#nanoTime()}.\n *\n * <p><b>Fail-open in the safe direction.</b> If the elapsed time already exceeds the floor, nothing\n * happens: a slow machine is not made slower. Padding is therefore never a correctness dependency,\n * only a leak-reduction measure - which is why the README lists timing as a residual risk rather than\n * claiming the side channel is closed. Park-and-hold is capped at {@link #MAX_SLEEP_MILLIS} per call\n * and never used to extend a refusal.\n *\n * <p><b>Not a throttle.</b> This never holds a request past the floor and never accumulates debt, so\n * it cannot be turned into a denial of service by driving up the measured cost.\n */\nfinal class LatencyBudget {\n\n    /** Ceiling on the floor, so a bad measurement cannot make sign-in com.example.auth;\n\nimport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\nimport java.security.MessageDigest;\nimport java.security.NoSuchAlgorithmException;\n\n/**\n * Renders the account field of an audit record.\n *\n * <p>The username is never written to the log. What is written instead is\n * {@code account_id=<64 hex chars>} - the SHA-256 of the submitted name, ASCII-lower-cased - plus\n * {@code account_hint=<distinct-codepoint-count>} and {@code account_present=true|false}.\n *\n * <p>Why this is safe to keep:\n * <ul>\n *   <li>It is one-way. A log reader cannot recover the name, so a leaked log is not a list of\n *       accounts. The hash is <em>not</em> keyed: an offline dictionary of candidate names can be\n *       hashed and compared, which is why the README lists unsalted account identifiers as residual\n *       risk and why the field exists at all - the alternative is a log that cannot be searched.</li>\n *   <li>It is computed from the <em>submitted</em> text in every code path, so it never depends on\n *       the outcome of the check and cannot tell an attacker anything they can also learn from the\n *       response.</li>\n *   <li>Lower-casing makes {@code Alice} and {@code alice} the same identifier, matching the\n *       store's one-record-per-name rule.</li>\n *   <li>The hint is a number in {@code 0..32}, derived only from the submitted text; it exists so\n *       an operator can tell \"one account probed many times\" from \"many names probed once\" without\n *       storing the names.</li>\n * </ul>\n */\nfinal class AccountField {\n\n    private static final char[] HEX = \"0123456789abcdef\".toCharArray();\n\n    private AccountField() {\n    }\n\n    static String render(String submittedUsername, boolean present) {\n        return \"account_id=\" + accountId(submittedUsername)\n                + \" account_hint=\" + distinctCodePoints(submittedUsername)\n                + \" account_present=\" + (present ? \"true\" : \"false\");\n    }\n\n    static String accountId(String username) {\n        if (username == null) {\n            return repeat('0', 64);\n        }\n        byte[] canonical = lowerAscii(username).getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n        final byte[] digest;\n        try {\n            digest = MessageDigest.getInstance(\"SHA-256\").digest(canonical);\n        } catch (NoSuchAlgorithmException e) {\n            // SHA-256 is required of every conformant JRE; reaching this means the platform is\n            // broken and the attempt must not be logged with a guess.\n            throw new IllegalStateException(e);\n        }\n        char[] out = new char[digest.length * 2];\n        for (int i = 0; i < digest.length; i++) {\n            int b = digest[i] & 0xff;\n            out[i * 2] = HEX[b >>> 4];\n            out[i * 2 + 1] = HEX[b & 0x0f];\n        }\n        return new String(out);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Renders the size hint: how many <em>distinct</em> code points the submitted name used.\n     *\n     * <p>The set is a fixed 256-bit sketch rather than an exact set, so it saturates at 32 and two\n     * very different names can share a value. The\n * suppressed cause is deliberate: this exception is constructed around keystore failures whose JDK\n * messages quote the keystore path.\n */\npublic final class InvalidConfigException extends Exception {\n\n    private static final long serialVersionUID = 1L;\n\n    private final List<String> problems;\n\n    InvalidConfigException(List<String> problems) {\n        super(describe(problems), null, false, false);\n        this.problems = Collections.unmodifiableList(new ArrayList<>(problems));\n    }\n\n    /** Every problem found, in the order detected. Safe to log; contains no configured values. */\n    public List<String> problems() {\n        return problems;\n    }\n\n    private static String describe(List<String> problems) {\n        if (problems.isEmpty()) {\n            return \"configuration is invalid\";\n        }\n        if (problems.size() == 1) {\n            return \"configuration is invalid: \" + problems.get(0);\n        }\n        return \"configuration is invalid (\" + problems.size() + \" problems): \" + String.join(\"; \", problems);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Renders a configuration key name defensively. Keys come from the environment and are\n     * attacker-chosen when the operator copies a template, so anything outside\n     * {@code A-Z0-9_} is dropped rather than printed - a key name must not become a log-injection\n     * or terminal-control vector.\n     */\n    static String describeKey(String key) {\n        if (key == null || key.isEmpty() || key.length() > 64) {\n            return \"an unsupported configuration key\";\n        }\n        for (int i = 0; i < key.length(); i++) {\n            char c = key.charAt(i);\n            boolean allowed = (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') || (c >= '0' && c <= '9') || c == '_';\n            if (!allowed) {\n                return \"an unsupported configuration key\";\n            }\n        }\n        return key;\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"user","uuid":"62a71560-1f5d-45c2-b550-57fc4906a3ed","session_id":"31967372-02de-4ae0-b5e8-f3ae53c5d120","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_76f1b6549ece492bb89db64c","is_error":false,"content":"package com.example.auth;\n\nimport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\nimport java.security.GeneralSecurityException;\nimport java.security.MessageDigest;\nimport java.security.SecureRandom;\nimport java.util.Arrays;\nimport java.util.Base64;\nimport javax.crypto.Mac;\nimport javax.crypto.SecretKeyFactory;\nimport javax.crypto.spec.PBEKeySpec;\nimport javax.crypto.spec.SecretKeySpec;\n\n/**\n * Computes and checks the stored credential digest.\n *\n * <p>Construction: {@code digest = HMAC-SHA-256(pepper, salt || PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA-256(password, salt, c))}\n * with a 16-byte per-account salt from {@link SecureRandom}.\n *\n * <p>Why this shape:\n * <ul>\n *   <li>PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA-256 from the JDK is used rather than a hand-rolled KDF and rather than\n *       SHA-256 or a single hash pass. The JDK has no built-in Argon2id or scrypt; the trade-off is\n *       documented in the README and the iteration floor is calibrated for it.</li>\n *   <li>The pepper is an HMAC <em>key</em>, not a string appended to the password. Concatenating a\n *       pepper and a password without length prefixes lets a short password and a longer one that\n *       absorbs part of the pepper collide; a MAC key has no such boundary.</li>\n *   <li>Comparison is {@link MessageDigest#isEqual(byte[], byte[])}, which is constant time.</li>\n *   <li>The salt is normalised through the JDK Base64 codec so the bytes that were hashed and the\n *       bytes re-derived at verification time are identical - no re-encoding drift.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <p>Intermediate material is wiped as soon as it is unused. Note that {@code char[]} contents\n * passed in are copied by {@link PBEKeySpec}; callers are responsible for wiping their own arrays,\n * and this class never retains a password.\n */\nfinal class PasswordHasher {\n\n    static final String KDF_ALGORITHM = \"PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256\";\n    static final String MAC_ALGORITHM = \"HmacSHA256\";\n    static final int SALT_BYTES = 16;\n    static final int DERIVED_KEY_BITS = 256;\n    static final int MIN_PEPPER_BYTES = 16;\n\n    /** Length of the stored digest, which is the HMAC-SHA-256 output. */\n    static final int MAX_DIGEST_BYTES = 32;\n\n    /** Upper bound on the work factor this class will perform in one call, independent of config. */\n    static final int MAX_ITERATIONS = 10_000_000;\n\n    private final byte[] pepper;\n    private final int iterations;\n\n    /**\n     * Hard ceiling on the work factor performed <em>per verification</em>, which is the configured\n     * count and not {@link #MAX_ITERATIONS}.\n     *\n     * <p>The iteration count is read from the credential file, so a record can order this class to do\n     * eighty times the work every other account costs - eighty times the CPU on the machine, and\n     * eighty times the delay before the latency floor releases. Two findings share that one number:\n     * the delay is an account-existence oracle that survives the uniform-work design (the equalising\n     * floor is measured against the configured cost, so a record that costs more than the floor is\n     * simply late), and it is a cheap denial of service against the four-permit work budget - a\n     * handful of such records holds every login slot. A record cannot legitimately ask for more work\n     * than the deployment's own current policy, so this class refuses it rather than performing it.\n     */\n    private final int maxSaltIterations;\n    private final SecureRandom random;\n\n    PasswordHasher(char[] pepperChars, int iterations, SecureRandom random) throws AuthException {\n        if (pepperChars == null || pepperChars.length < MIN_PEPPER_BYTES || iterations < 1) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        if (iterations > MAX_ITERATIONS) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        byte[] encoded = InputValidator.encodePassword(pepperChars);\n        if (encoded.length < MIN_PEPPER_BYTES) {\n            Arrays.fill(encoded, (byte) 0);\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        this.pepper = encoded;\n        this.iterations = iterations;\n        this.maxSaltIterations = iterations;\n        this.random = random;\n    }\n\n    byte[] newSalt() {\n        byte[] salt = new byte[SALT_BYTES];\n        random.nextBytes(salt);\n        return salt;\n    }\n\n    int iterations() {\n        return iterations;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Derives the digest to store.\n     *\n     * @param passwordUtf8 UTF-8 bytes of the password, already validated for exact encodability\n     */\n    byte[] derive(byte[] passwordUtf8, byte[] salt, int saltIterations) throws AuthException {\n        if (salt == null || salt.length != SALT_BYTES) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        if (saltIterations < 1 || saltIterations > maxSaltIterations) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        if (passwordUtf8 == null || passwordUtf8.length == 0) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT);\n        }\n        // PBEKeySpec takes characters, so the validated UTF-8 bytes are turned back into the\n        // ISO-8859-1 view of the same bytes. This is a bijection, so it cannot merge two distinct\n        // passwords - unlike the platform default charset, which folds unrepresentable characters.\n        char[] asChars = iso88591Chars(passwordUtf8);\n        PBEKeySpec spec = new PBEKeySpec(asChars, salt, saltIterations, DERIVED_KEY_BITS);\n        Arrays.fill(asChars, (char) 0);\n        final byte[] derived;\n        try {\n            SecretKeyFactory factory = SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(KDF_ALGORITHM);\n            derived = factory.generateSecret(spec).getEncoded();\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException | RuntimeException e) {\n            spec.clearPassword();\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        spec.clearPassword();\n        try {\n            Mac mac = Mac.getInstance(MAC_ALGORITHM);\n            mac.init(new SecretKeySpec(pepper, MAC_ALGORITHM));\n            mac.update(salt);\n            byte[] digest = mac.doFinal(derived);\n            Arrays.fill(derived, (byte) 0);\n            return digest;\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException | RuntimeException e) {\n            Arrays.fill(derived, (byte) 0);\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Checks a candidate password against a stored digest in constant time with respect to the\n     * digest contents.\n     *\n     * @return {@code true} only when the candidate reproduces the stored digest\n     */\n    boolean matches(byte[] passwordUtf8, byte[] salt, int saltIterations, byte[] storedDigest) throws AuthException {\n        if (storedDigest == null || storedDigest.length != MAX_DIGEST_BYTES) {\n            // A truncated or oversized stored digest cannot be checked; treat as a store problem\n            // so the attempt fails closed instead of comparing against a padded value.\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        byte[] candidate = derive(passwordUtf8, salt, saltIterations);\n        try {\n            return MessageDigest.isEqual(candidate, storedDigest);\n        } finally {\n            Arrays.fill(candidate, (byte) 0);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Decodes a Base64 field from the credential file, requiring the exact canonical form so that\n     * two different textual values can never denote the same salt.\n     *\n     * <p>There is deliberately no matching encoder. This component reads credential records and\n     * never writes them: rendering a record is provisioning, which is account creation, and outside\n     * the scope of an authentication component.\n     *\n     * @return the decoded bytes, or {@code null} when the text is not canonical Base64\n     */\n    static byte[] decodeBytes(String value, int expectedLength) {\n        if (value == null || value.isEmpty()) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        final byte[] decoded;\n        try {\n            decoded = Base64.getDecoder().decode(value);\n        } catch (IllegalArgumentException e) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (decoded.length != expectedLength) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (!Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(decoded).equals(value)) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        return decoded;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Measures how long {@code iterations} of PBKDF2 take on this machine, used by the latency\n     * budget in {@link CredentialVerifier} so the budget tracks the configured work factor instead\n     * of a hard-coded guess.\n     */\n    long measureDerivationNanos(int iterations, byte[] probePassword) {\n        byte[] salt = newSalt();\n        long best = Long.MAX_VALUE;\n        for (int i = 0; i < 2; i++) {\n            long start = System.nanoTime();\n            try {\n                derive(probePassword, salt, iterations);\n            } catch (AuthException e) {\n                return -1L;\n            }\n            long elapsed = System.nanoTime() - start;\n            if (elapsed < best) {\n                best = elapsed;\n            }\n        }\n        return best;\n    }\n\n    void erase() {\n        Arrays.fill(pepper, (byte) 0);\n    }\n\n    private static char[] iso88591Chars(byte[] bytes) {\n        char[] out = new char[bytes.length];\n        for (int i = 0; i < bytes.length; i++) {\n            out[i] = (char) (bytes[i] & 0xff);\n        }\n        return out;\n    }\n\n    static byte[] utf8(String value) {\n        return value.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"9b333bfb-f0bb-42e8-9085-b1d3f35607db","session_id":"31967372-02de-4ae0-b5e8-f3ae53c5d120","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"9b333bfb-f0bb-42e8-9085-b1d3f35607db","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Interesting! com.example.auth;\n\nimport java.util.concurrent.Semaphore;\nimport java.util.concurrent.TimeUnit;\n\n/**\n * Checks one credential pair and shapes the work it does so that no failure outcome is\n * distinguishable from another by timing.\n *\n * <p><b>Comparable work for every outcome.</b> Each of these paths performs one full PBKDF2\n * derivation before it returns:\n * <ul>\n *   <li>username not in the store - the derivation uses a server-generated salt with the configured\n *       iteration count, and its result is discarded;</li>\n *   <li>username present, password wrong;</li>\n *   <li>username present, password correct.</li>\n * </ul>\n * After the derivation, every remaining difference is a field read or a map probe, and the latency\n * floor absorbs the residual spread.\n *\n * <p><b>Work is capped globally, not per key.</b> A fixed number of permits, handed out in FIFO\n * order, bounds how much hashing an attacker can force regardless of how many usernames or source\n * addresses the attempts are spread across. A caller that cannot obtain a permit within\n * {@value #PERMIT_TIMEOUT_MILLIS} ms is denied; attempts are never queued without bound, because an\n * unbounded queue is the denial of service this exists to prevent. Waiting for a permit also counts\n * against the latency floor, so a saturated service is uniformly slower - it cannot be made to run\n * one outcome fast and another slow.\n *\n * <p><b>No latch.</b> Saturating the permits denies only the attempts that arrive while saturated;\n * permits are released in a {@code finally} block and the next attempt proceeds normally.\n */\nfinal class CredentialVerifier {\n\n    /** Longest a caller will wait for a work permit before being denied. */\n    static final long PERMIT_TIMEOUT_MILLIS = 2_000L;\n\n    private final PasswordHasher hasher;\n    private final LatencyBudget budget;\n    private final Semaphore workPermits;\n    private final int maxConcurrentDerivations;\n    private final AttemptParameters decoy;\n\n    CredentialVerifier(PasswordHasher hasher, LatencyBudget budget, int maxConcurrentDerivations,\n            AttemptParameters decoy) {\n        if (maxConcurrentDerivations < 1) {\n            throw new IllegalArgumentException(\"maxConcurrentDerivations\");\n        }\n        this.hasher = hasher;\n        this.budget = budget;\n        this.workPermits = new Semaphore(maxConcurrentDerivations, true);\n        this.maxConcurrentDerivations = maxConcurrentDerivations;\n        this.decoy = decoy;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Verifies a password against the record for a resolved username.\n     *\n     * @param passwordUtf8 validated UTF-8 bytes of the candidate password\n     * @param record the record for the resolved username, or {@code null} when there is none\n     * @return {@code true} only when the digest matches\n     * @throws AuthException when the check could not be performed at all - no permit, no decoy\n     *     parameters, or a store failure. The caller must deny the attempt and must not treat this\n     *     as either a match or a distinguishable kind of failure.\n     */\n    boolean verify(byte[] passwordUtf8, AccountCredentials record) throws AuthException {\n        if (passwordUtf8 == null || passwordUtf8.length == 0) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT);\n        }\n        if (decoy == null) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        boolean acquired;\n        try {\n            acquired = workPermits.tryAcquire(PERMIT_TIMEOUT_MILLIS, TimeUnit.MILLISECONDS);\n        } catch (InterruptedException e) {\n            Thread.currentThread().interrupt();\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.WORK_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        if (!acquired) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.WORK_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        long started = System.nanoTime();\n        try {\n            final boolean matched;\n            if (record == null) {\n                // Discarded deliberately: the purpose is that an unknown username costs exactly what\n                // a known one costs.\n                hasher.matches(passwordUtf8, decoy.salt(), decoy.iterations(), decoy.digest());\n                matched = false;\n            } else {\n                matched = hasher.matches(passwordUtf8, record.salt(), record.iterations(), record.digest());\n            }\n            budget.padUntil(started);\n            return matched;\n        } finally {\n            workPermits.release();\n        }\n    }\n\n    /** Derivations the service will run at once. */\n    int maxConcurrentDerivations() {\n        return maxConcurrentDerivations;\n    }\n\n    /** Salt, iteration count and digest used to burn work for an unknown username. */\n    static final class AttemptParameters {\n\n        private final byte[] salt;\n        private final int iterations;\n        private final byte[] digest;\n\n        AttemptParameters(byte[] salt, int iterations, byte[] digest) {\n            this.salt = salt;\n            this.iterations = iterations;\n            this.digest = digest;\n        }\n\n        byte[] salt() {\n            return salt.clone();\n        }\n\n        int iterations() {\n            return iterations;\n        }\n\n        byte[] digest() {\n            return digest.clone();\n        }\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"a8012be1-08e7-4432-8963-aedce9fb5ae7","session_id":"31967372-02de-4ae0-b5e8-f3ae53c5d120","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"a8012be1-08e7-4432-8963-aedce9fb5ae7","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Next,
