com.example.auth;\n\nimport java.util.ArrayDeque;\nimport java.util.ArrayList;\nimport java.util.List;\nimport java.util.concurrent.ConcurrentHashMap;\nimport java.util.concurrent.atomic.AtomicLong;\n\n/**\n * Per-account lockout and per-source rate limiting, applied to the same attempt.\n *\n * <p><b>Two budgets, deliberately independent.</b> A single limit keyed on the account lets an\n * attacker who knows one username spend that account's whole budget and lock its real owner out; a\n * single limit keyed on the source lets an attacker with many usernames exhaust one shared NAT or\n * proxy address. Both are needed and neither may be reachable by the other's traffic:\n * <ul>\n *   <li>the per-account budget only ever counts failures against <em>that</em> account;</li>\n *   <li>the per-source window counts <em>all</em> attempts from that address and is configured, by\n *       {@link AuthConfig}, to be at least the account lock threshold, so one targeted account\n *       cannot on its own exhaust a shared address's budget. {@code sourceMaxAttempts} must be set\n *       well above {@code accountLockAfter} in any deployment that serves shared addresses - the\n *       README gives the sizing rule.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <p><b>Check and act are one operation.</b> Every verdict is produced inside a single\n * {@link ConcurrentHashMap#compute(Object, java.util.function.BiFunction)} call, which the map runs\n * atomically for that key. There is no \"read the count, decide, then write it back\" window in which\n * two concurrent attempts could both be allowed, and no separate lock that could be held across the\n * credential check.\n *\n * <p><b>Bounded and fail-closed.</b> Both maps are capped. When a cap is reached the table is swept\n * for keys that have been idle longer than the longest window; if the table is still full the\n * verdict for a new key is {@code BLOCKED} rather than \"untracked\", because an untracked key is an\n * unlimited key. An attacker cannot grow this memory without bound by spraying usernames.\n *\n * <p>State is keyed on server-computed values only: the canonical username resolved from the store\n * (never the submitted spelling) and the canonical address produced by\n * {@link InputValidator#normalizeSourceAddress}.\n *\n * <p>Residual risk, stated in the README: this state is per process. Behind a load balancer the\n * budgets are per node, so the effective limit is the configured limit times the node count; a\n * deployment needing a shared budget must enforce it in a shared store, and this component's limits\n * remain as the last line of defence.\n */\nfinal class AttemptThrottle {\n\n    /** Cap on tracked account keys. */\n    static final int MAX_ACCOUNT_KEYS = 20_000;\n\n    /** Cap on tracked source keys. */\n    static final int MAX_SOURCE_KEYS = 20_000;\n\n    /** Operations between sweeps for idle keys. */\n    private static final int SWEEP_INTERVAL = 64;\n\n    private final ConcurrentHashMap<String, AccountBucket> accounts = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n    private final ConcurrentHashMap<String, SourceBucket> sources = new ConcurrentHashMap<>();\n    private final AtomicLong operations = new AtomicLong();\n\n    /** Verdict of a throttle check. */\n    enum Kind {\n\n        /** Proceed with the credential check. */\n        ALLOW,\n\n        /** Do not proceed; the budget for this key is exhausted. */\n        BLOCKED,\n\n        /** Do not proceed; the key's temporary lock is in force. */\n        LOCKED\n    }\n\n    /** Immutable verdict, carrying the retry hint when the answer is no. */\n    static final class Decision {\n\n        private final Kind kind;\n        private final int retryAfterSeconds;\n\n        Decision(Kind kind, int retryAfterSeconds) {\n            this.kind = kind;\n            this.retryAfterSeconds = retryAfterSeconds;\n        }\n\n        Kind kind() {\n            return kind;\n        }\n\n        int retryAfterSeconds() {\n            return retryAfterSeconds;\n        }\n\n        boolean allowed() {\n            return kind == Kind.ALLOW;\n        }\n\n        @Override\n        public String toString() {\n            return \"Decision[\" + kind + \", retryAfterSeconds=\" + retryAfterSeconds + \"]\";\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static final Decision ALLOW = new Decision(Kind.ALLOW, 0);\n\n    /**\n     * The throttle key for a submitted name: ASCII case folded, nothing else.\n     *\n     * <p>Keying on the <em>submitted</em> name rather than on a resolved record is deliberate. If\n     * the key existed only after a successful lookup, an attacker flooding names that do not exist\n     * would create no state at all and would face no per-name accounting; keying on the submitted\n     * name means the flood is accounted to the name it targets. Because only ASCII case is folded,\n     * this cannot fold two genuinely different accounts together - the store has already refused to\n     * load if two records differ only by ASCII case - and it cannot fold a Unicode look-alike onto a\n     * real account, which would let an attacker lock a victim out by attacking a similar name.\n     */\n    static String canonicalize(String submitted) {\n        if (submitted == null) {\n            return \"\";\n        }\n        StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder(Math.min(submitted.length(), 128));\n        int limit = Math.min(submitted.length(), 128);\n        for (int i = 0; i < limit; i++) {\n            char c = submitted.charAt(i);\n            if (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') {\n                c = (char) (c + 32);\n            }\n            out.append(c);\n        }\n        return out.toString();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Consumes one attempt from the source budget and applies its lock.\n     *\n     * <p>Called before any credential work, so an attacker cannot buy a full PBKDF2 round per\n     * attempt beyond their budget.\n     */\n    Decision consumeSourceAttempt(String canonicalSource, long nowMillis, int windowSeconds, int maxAttempts,\n            int blockSeconds) {\n        sweepIfDue(nowMillis);\n        List<Decision> observed = new ArrayList<>(1);\n        sources.compute(canonicalSource, (key, existing) -> {\n            SourceBucket bucket = existing;\n            long horizon = nowMillis - windowSeconds * 1000L;\n            if (bucket == null) {\n                if (sources.size() >= MAX_SOURCE_KEYS && !evictIdleSources(horizon)) {\n                    observed.add(new Decision(Kind.BLOCKED, blockSeconds));\n                    return null;\n                }\n                bucket = new SourceBucket();\n            }\n            synchronized (bucket) {\n                bucket.lastTouchedMillis = nowMillis;\n                if (bucket.blockedUntilMillis > nowMillis) {\n                    observed.add(new Decision(Kind.BLOCKED, secondsUntil(bucket.blockedUntilMillis, nowMillis)));\n                    return bucket;\n                }\n                ArrayDeque<Long> stamps = bucket.attempts;\n                while (!stamps.isEmpty() && stamps.peekFirst() < horizon) {\n                    stamps.pollFirst();\n                }\n                if (stamps.size() >= maxAttempts) {\n                    bucket.blockedUntilMillis = nowMillis + blockSeconds * 1000L;\n                    observed.add(new Decision(Kind.BLOCKED, blockSeconds));\n                    return bucket;\n                }\n                stamps.addLast(nowMillis);\n                observed.add(ALLOW);\n                return bucket;\n            }\n        });\n        return observed.isEmpty() ? blockedForever() : observed.get(0);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Applies the per-account lock, without consuming anything.\n     *\n     * <p>Read-only with respect to the budget: an attacker cannot lengthen another party's lock, and\n     * probing a locked account does not extend the lock either.\n     */\n    Decision checkAccountLock(String canonicalUsername, long nowMillis) {\n        AccountBucket bucket = accounts.get(canonicalUsername);\n        if (bucket == null) {\n            return ALLOW;\n        }\n        synchronized (bucket) {\n            if (bucket.blockedUntilMillis > nowMillis) {\n                return new Decision(Kind.LOCKED, secondsUntil(bucket.blockedUntilMillis, nowMillis));\n            }\n            return ALLOW;\n        }\n The increment and the lock decision happen in the same {@code compute} call, so two\n     * concurrent failures cannot both see \"one below the limit\" and let a third through.\n     */\n    Decision recordAccountFailure(String canonicalUsername, long nowMillis, int lockAfter, int lockSeconds) {\n        sweepIfDue(nowMillis);\n        List<Decision> observed = new ArrayList<>(1);\n        accounts.compute(canonicalUsername, (key, existing) -> {\n            AccountBucket bucket = existing;\n            if (bucket == null) {\n                if (accounts.size() >= MAX_ACCOUNT_KEYS && !evictIdleAccounts(nowMillis - lockSeconds * 2000L)) {\n                    observed.add(new Decision(Kind.BLOCKED, lockSeconds));\n                    return null;\n                }\n                bucket = new AccountBucket();\n            }\n            synchronized (bucket) {\n                bucket.lastTouchedMillis = nowMillis;\n                if (bucket.blockedUntilMillis > nowMillis) {\n                    observed.add(new Decision(Kind.LOCKED, secondsUntil(bucket.blockedUntilMillis, nowMillis)));\n                    return bucket;\n                }\n                bucket.consecutiveFailures++;\n                if (bucket.consecutiveFailures >= lockAfter) {\n                    bucket.blockedUntilMillis = nowMillis + lockSeconds * 1000L;\n                    bucket.consecutiveFailures = 0;\n                    observed.add(new Decision(Kind.LOCKED, lockSeconds));\n                    return bucket;\n                }\n                observed.add(ALLOW);\n                return bucket;\n            }\n        });\n        return observed.isEmpty() ? blockedForever() : observed.get(0);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Clears an account's failure state after a verified sign-in.\n     *\n     * <p>Clearing on success rather than on window expiry is what stops a lockout from being a\n     * permanent handle: an attacker cannot keep an account locked by generating failures around the\n     * owner's successful sign-ins, because the owner's success resets the streak.\n     */\n    void resetAccount(String canonicalUsername) {\n        accounts.computeIfPresent(canonicalUsername, (key, bucket) -> {\n            synchronized (bucket) {\n                bucket.consecutiveFailures = 0;\n                bucket.blockedUntilMillis = 0L;\n            }\n            return bucket;\n        });\n    }\n\n    /** Number of keys currently tracked; used by the deployment check and by tests. */\n    int trackedAccountKeys() {\n        return accounts.size();\n    }\n\n    int trackedSourceKeys() {\n        return sources.size();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Drops keys idle longer than the sweep horizon.\n     *\n     * <p>The clock passed in is the same one every verdict is computed from. Reading\n     * {@link System#currentTimeMillis()} here instead would compare the injected timeline against\n     * the wall clock: wherever the two differ - a test clock, or a deployment whose clock was stepped\n     * after buckets were created - the sweep would see every bucket as idle and delete the lockout\n     * state while authentication kept reporting healthy limits.\n     */\n    private void sweepIfDue(long nowMillis) {\n        long tick = operations.incrementAndGet();\n        if (tick % SWEEP_INTERVAL != 0) {\n            return;\n        }\n        long idleHorizon = nowMillis - SWEEP_INTERVAL_IDLE_MILLIS;\n        accounts.entrySet().removeIf(entry -> {\n            AccountBucket bucket = entry.getValue();\n            synchronized (bucket) {\n                return bucket.lastTouchedMillis < idleHorizon && bucket.blockedUntilMillis <= nowMillis;\n            }\n        });\n        sources.entrySet().removeIf(entry -> {\n            SourceBucket bucket = entry.getValue();\n            synchronized (bucket) {\n                // Stamps older than the longest window the configuration accepts can no longer\n                // affect any verdict, so they are dropped here. Without this a bucket keeps its\n                // stamps forever - and a bucket with stamps is never idle enough to sweep - so an\n                // attacker who spends {@code sourceMaxAttempts} attempts from each of\n                // {@code MAX_SOURCE_KEYS} addresses leaves the process holding every one of those\n                // timestamps permanently.\n                bucket.pruneStampsBefore(nowMillis - MAX_WINDOW_MILLIS);\n                return bucket.attempts.isEmpty() && bucket.blockedUntilMillis <= nowMillis\n                        && bucket.lastTouchedMillis < idleHorizon;\n            }\n        });\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * The longest per-source window the configuration bounds allow, plus a margin. Pruning at this\n     * horizon is always at least as generous as any legal {@code sourceWindowSeconds}, so it can\n     * never discard a stamp that a verdict would still have counted.\n     */\n    private static final long MAX_WINDOW_MILLIS = (AuthConfig.MAX_WINDOW_SECONDS + 300L) * 1000L;\n\n    /** Idle horizon: longer than the longest configurable window (one hour) plus margin. */\n    private static final long SWEEP_INTERVAL_IDLE_MILLIS = 90 * 60 * 1000L;\n\n    private boolean evictIdleAccounts(long horizon) {\n        int before = accounts.size();\n        accounts.entrySet().removeIf(entry -> {\n            AccountBucket bucket = entry.getValue();\n            synchronized (bucket) {\n                return bucket.lastTouchedMillis < horizon;\n            }\n        });\n        return accounts.size() < before;\n    }\n\n    private boolean evictIdleSources(long horizon) {\n        int before = sources.size();\n        sources.entrySet().removeIf(entry -> {\n            SourceBucket bucket = entry.getValue();\n            synchronized (bucket) {\n                return bucket.lastTouchedMillis < horizon;\n            }\n        });\n        return sources.size() < before;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Verdict when the tracking table is full and nothing could be evicted. Refusing is the only\n     * fail-closed answer: reporting \"allowed\" for an untracked key would make the cap a way to\n     * obtain unlimited attempts.\n     */\n    private static Decision blockedForever() {\n        return new Decision(Kind.BLOCKED, MAX_RETRY_AFTER_SECONDS);\n    }\n\n    /** Upper bound on the advertised retry hint, so it stays usable as a {@code Retry-After}. */\n    static final int MAX_RETRY_AFTER_SECONDS = 3600;\n\n    private static int secondsUntil(long untilMillis, long nowMillis) {\n        long delta = untilMillis - nowMillis;\n        if (delta <= 0) {\n            return 1;\n        }\n        long seconds = (delta + 999L) / 1000L;\n        return (int) Math.min(seconds, MAX_RETRY_AFTER_SECONDS);\n    }\n\n    private static final class AccountBucket {\n\n        private int consecutiveFailures;\n        private long blockedUntilMillis;\n        private long lastTouchedMillis = System.currentTimeMillis();\n\n        private AccountBucket() {\n        }\n    }\n\n    private static final class SourceBucket {\n\n        private final ArrayDeque<Long> attempts = new ArrayDeque<>();\n        private long blockedUntilMillis;\n        private long lastTouchedMillis = System.currentTimeMillis();\n\n        private SourceBucket() {\n        }\n\n        /** Drops stamps that no legal window could still count. Callers hold the bucket monitor. */\n        private void pruneStampsBefore(long horizon) {\n            while (!attempts.isEmpty() && attempts.peekFirst() < horizon) {\n                attempts.pollFirst();\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"user","uuid":"2195731d-8d77-4254-99fd-5cc0d2ab6d00","session_id":"31967372-02de-4ae0-b5e8-f3ae53c5d120","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_55dfcce63ba648d5a5184a78","is_error":false,"content":"package com.example.auth;\n\nimport java.net.Inet4Address;\nimport java.net.Inet6Address;\nimport java.net.InetAddress;\nimport java.net.UnknownHostException;\nimport java.nio.ByteBuffer;\nimport java.nio.CharBuffer;\nimport java.nio.charset.CharsetEncoder;\nimport java.nio.charset.CoderResult;\nimport java.nio.charset.CodingErrorAction;\nimport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\nimport java.util.ArrayList;\nimport java.util.Collections;\nimport java.util.List;\n\n/**\n * Textual input validation for the two client-controlled strings this component accepts.\n *\n * <p>Both checks are fail-closed: anything not explicitly allowed is rejected, there is no\n * normalisation that could fold two different inputs onto the same account, and no rejection ever\n * echoes the offending value.\n */\npublic final class InputValidator {\n\n    /** Longest username accepted. */\n    public static final int MAX_USERNAME_CODEPOINTS = 64;\n\n    /**\n     * Shortest username accepted. (byte) 0);\n        if (encoded.length == 0) {\n            java.util.Arrays.fill(encoded, (byte) 0);\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT);\n        }\n        return OK. `blockSeconds * 1000L` OK. `lockSeconds * 2000L` OK.\n\n- checkAccountLock: reads `accounts.get`, synchronizes on bucket; races with sweep's removeIf: sweep also synchronizes on bucket; removal from map concurrent with verdict — verdict computed on a bucket that may be being removed. Worst case: an ALLOW verdict on a bucket whose blockedUntil has just expired; or LOCKED verdict on a bucket being removed — but sweep only removes when `blockedUntilMillis <= nowMillis` (not locked), so no contradiction. OK.\n\nrecordAccountFailure: evictIdleAccounts horizon = nowMillis - lockSeconds*2000L. evictIdleAccounts removes based on lastTouched < horizon regardless of blockedUntil! Look: evictIdleAccounts's removeIf only checks `bucket.lastTouchedMillis < horizon` — unlike sweepIfDue which also requires blockedUntilMillis <= nowMillis, it does not check blockedUntil. Horizon = now - 2*lockSeconds… so a locked bucket (blockedUntil = failure-time + lockSeconds) whose lastTouched is… if lastTouched < now - 2*lockSeconds, then blockedUntil = lastTouched + lockSeconds < now - lockSeconds < now. So at that horizon, the lock has already expired. OK — the 2× multiplier guarantees eviction only targets expired locks. No problem.\n\nevictIdleSources horizon = nowMillis - windowSeconds*1000L. A blocked source has blockedUntil = lastTouched + blockSeconds. If lastTouched < now - windowSeconds*1000 then… blockedUntil = lastTouched + blockSeconds*1000. If blockSeconds > windowSeconds, blockedUntil could still be > now! Example: window=60s, block=3600s. Source got blocked 3500 seconds ago; lastTouched=now-3500s; horizon = now-60s; lastTouched < horizon → evicted despite being blocked until now+100s. The blocked record is removed → the source gets a fresh bucket on next attempt → allowed! Is this a real flaw? When a source exceeds maxAttempts, blockedUntil = now + blockSeconds is set. On subsequent attempts while blocked, the early return in consumeSourceAttempt `if (bucket.blockedUntilMillis > nowMillis) { observed.add(BLOCKED...); return bucket; }` — but note: `bucket.lastTouchedMillis = nowMillis;` is assigned before the block check. So every blocked attempt updates lastTouched. Therefore, a blocked source that keeps hammering keeps lastTouched current and won't be evicted (lastTouched = now ≥ horizon). If it stops hammering, the block is irrelevant… it doesn't come back. Hmm, so what's evicted is: a source that got blocked and hasn't returned within windowSeconds since last touch. If it returns after windowSeconds but before blockSeconds, eviction removes the block early! Timeline: source hammering at t=0 → blocked until t+3600 (blockSeconds=3600, window=60). Last attempt at t=0 (or does it retry? each retry updates lastTouched). Attacker stops for 61 seconds. At t=61, sweep only runs on operations%64… the sweep horizon is 90 minutes so sweep won't remove it (90 min > block). But evictIdleSources only runs when the table is full (sources.size() >= MAX_SOURCE_KEYS). So an attacker would need to fill 20,000 source keys to trigger eviction. Attack: attacker floods to create 20,000 source buckets (each with 1 attempt… lastTouched gets updated…). Then, when a blocked source returns at t=61s: consumeSourceAttempt → bucket exists (blocked) → compute is called with existing != null → the eviction path isn't taken (eviction is only on bucket == null). Wait, eviction only happens when the new source's bucket is null. If the blocked bucket exists, no eviction. The evictIdleSources call is only made to make space for new keys. The removeIf during that call removes any source with lastTouched < horizon = now - windowSeconds*1000 — including blocked ones whose block hasn't expired (last touched more than windowSeconds ago, and blockSeconds > windowSeconds). So: attacker's source S gets blocked at t=0 (lastTouched=t0… but wait, each blocked attempt updates lastTouched to now — so attacker keeps sending on S to keep lastTouched fresh — no wait, if the attacker keeps sending, lastTouched stays near now and won't be < horizon. To get evicted, S must not send for >windowSeconds. Then attacker (from another key) triggers the fill and comes back with S: S's bucket==null? No — S's bucket was evicted during someone else's fill attempt. Then S comes back with a new attempt: bucket==null, table still full? evictIdleSources(horizon) gets called → returns true (removed someone including S's old blocked bucket… wait, S's bucket was already removed during the earlier fill). Then S creates a new bucket → ALLOW → attacker gets attempts despite being blocked. So this is a real early-block-release path: blocked sources can escape their block by (a) waiting longer than windowSeconds without touching, and (b) ensuring some new source's attempt triggers an eviction sweep when the table is full. But wait — evictIdleSources only runs when `sources.size() >= MAX_SOURCE_KEYS`. The attacker needs 20,000 keys — which is possible (the cap itself allows 20k keys, each needing 1 attempt). The attacker can easily maintain 20k keys active. So exploitability: an attacker who filled the table can effectively shorten the source block to windowSeconds (idle) — no actually simpler: while the table is full, any new source's attempt triggers evictIdleSources, removing all sources idle more than windowSeconds — including blocked ones. A blocked source that pauses beyond windowSeconds and then returns gets a fresh budget. The block effectively only lasts windowSeconds of silence. This undermines the source block duration when blockSeconds > windowSeconds and the table is full. Severity: Medium? Requires table-full condition (attacker can force by spraying 20k source IPs — requires botnet-scale distinct IPs… 20k distinct IPs is a lot but plausible with a proxy mesh/botnet). And config typically has blockSeconds > windowSeconds. Hmm, but is this really a flaw or accepted design? The sweepIfDue comment explicitly guards blocked buckets (`&& bucket.blockedUntilMillis <= nowMillis`) — the same author intentionally added that guard to the 90-min sweep but not to evictIdleSources/evictIdleAccounts. This asymmetry suggests a planted bug: evictIdleSources/evictIdleAccounts lack the blockedUntil guard. Let me re-check evictIdleAccounts: horizon = now - lockSeconds*2000; a locked bucket's blockedUntil = lastTouch + lockSeconds*1000 at failure time; lastTouched also updates during lock (checkAccountLock doesn't touch lastTouched — it's read-only! And recordAccountFailure updates but returns LOCKED early… recordAccountFailure does update lastTouchedMillis = nowMillis even when blocked: the sequence is synchronized { bucket.lastTouchedMillis = nowMillis; if (blockedUntil > now) { LOCKED; return }...}. But checkAccountLock is read-only and occurs first in the attempt ordering: consumeSourceAttempt (source update) → checkAccountLock (no account update) → … if the account is locked, authenticate returns rateLimited right after checkAccountLock — recordAccountFailure is never reached! So during lock, probe attempts don't update the account bucket's lastTouchedMillis (only the source bucket). Therefore the account's lastTouched stays at the last failure before lock engagement. The lock lasts lockSeconds. Eviction horizon = now - 2*lockSeconds. lastTouched = time of lock engagement = now - elapsed. Eviction at elapsed > 2*lockSeconds ⇒ lock expired (lock ends at elapsed = lockSeconds). So the account side is safe: eviction only after lock expired, 2× margin. Clever. Account OK.\n\nSource side: consumeSourceAttempt updates lastTouched on every attempt, including blocked ones (assignment before block check). Blocked source keeps hammering → stays. Stops for >windowSeconds → evictable while block is still in force (when blockSeconds > windowSeconds). The equivalent margin for sources would be to not evict blocked buckets (or horizon ≥ blockSeconds). windowSeconds*1000 is used as the horizon; blockSeconds is independent, up to… AuthConfig's bounds: MAX_WINDOW_SECONDS (1 hour?) and block seconds probably similarly up to an hour. blockSeconds > windowSeconds is a typical configuration. So this is a genuine flaw: per-source block can be cut short when the key table is under pressure — the fail-closed claim (\"if still full, verdict for new keys is BLOCKED\") is preserved, but eviction releases existing blocked buckets. CWE-340? Better: CWE-613 (Insufficient Session Expiration)-ish? This is premature expiration of a security state (rate-limit block). CWE-672? Hmm. CWE-613 is about sessions. Might map to CWE-340 (Generation of Predictable or Responsive Identification Sequence)… no. Let me think: expiration of a security mechanism — \"CWE-1387: Flexible Time/Weight-Based Restrictions with Inadequate Granularity\"? Obscure. Common mapping for lockout bypass is CWE-307 (Improper Restriction of Excessive Authentication Attempts)! Yes — this finding is a partial bypass of excessive-attempt restriction: a blocked source can regain attempts before the advertised block expires. CWE-307 is the right frame. Severity: Medium (requires attacker to fill 20k source keys; the result shortens the source block to the idle window; per-account lockout is unaffected; attacker still consumes budget, but…). Wait, actually — let me reconfirm eviction really does remove blocked buckets: evictIdleSources removeIf: `synchronized (bucket) { return bucket.lastTouchedMillis < horizon; }` — yes, no blockedUntil check. Confirmed.\n\nBut wait — to reach evictIdleSources, the new key's bucket must be null AND size ≥ MAX. Also: is the removal of blocked sources reachable even when the table isn't full? No. So preconditions: sources map is full (attacker-fillable) + blocked source idle beyond windowSeconds. Then the blocked source returns → fresh bucket → full budget again. The attacker only needed to stay silent for windowSeconds (e.g. 5 min) rather than blockSeconds (e.g. 1 hour). This effectively reduces the source block duration to windowSeconds for an attacker who can keep the table full. Real Medium finding.\n\nHmm wait, let me also verify the other direction — evictIdleSources returns true if any removal happened, allowing the new key to be inserted. Even if only blocked ones are removed. com.example.auth;\n\nimport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\nimport java.security.GeneralSecurityException;\nimport java.security.MessageDigest;\nimport java.security.SecureRandom;\nimport java.util.Arrays;\nimport java.util.Base64;\nimport javax.crypto.Mac;\nimport javax.crypto.SecretKeyFactory;\nimport javax.crypto.spec.PBEKeySpec;\nimport javax.crypto.spec.SecretKeySpec;\n\n/**\n * Computes and checks the stored credential digest.\n *\n * <p>Construction: {@code digest = HMAC-SHA-256(pepper, salt || PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA-256(password, salt, c))}\n * with a 16-byte per-account salt from {@link SecureRandom}.\n *\n * <p>Why this shape:\n * <ul>\n *   <li>PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA-256 from the JDK is used rather than a hand-rolled KDF and rather than\n *       SHA-256 or a single hash pass. The JDK has no built-in Argon2id or scrypt; the trade-off is\n *       documented in the README and the iteration floor is calibrated for it.</li>\n *   <li>The pepper is an HMAC <em>key</em>, not a string appended to the password. Concatenating a\n *       pepper and a password without length prefixes lets a short password and a longer one that\n *       absorbs part of the pepper collide; a MAC key has no such boundary.</li>\n *   <li>Comparison is {@link MessageDigest#isEqual(byte[], byte[])}, which is constant time.</li>\n *   <li>The salt is normalised through the JDK Base64 codec so the bytes that were hashed and the\n *       bytes re-derived at verification time are identical - no re-encoding drift.</li>\n * </ul>\n *\n * <p>Intermediate material is wiped as soon as it is unused. Note that {@code char[]} contents\n * passed in are copied by {@link PBEKeySpec}; callers are responsible for wiping their own arrays,\n * and this class never retains a password.\n */\nfinal class PasswordHasher {\n\n    static final String KDF_ALGORITHM = \"PBKDF2WithHmacSHA256\";\n    static final String MAC_ALGORITHM = \"HmacSHA256\";\n    static final int SALT_BYTES = 16;\n    static final int DERIVED_KEY_BITS = 256;\n    static final int MIN_PEPPER_BYTES = 16;\n\n    /** Length of the stored digest, which is the HMAC-SHA-256 output. */\n    static final int MAX_DIGEST_BYTES = 32;\n\n    /** Upper bound on the work factor this class will perform in one call, independent of config. */\n    static final int MAX_ITERATIONS = 10_000_000;\n\n    private final byte[] pepper;\n    private final int iterations;\n\n    /**\n     * Hard ceiling on the work factor performed <em>per verification</em>, which is the configured\n     * count and not {@link #MAX_ITERATIONS}.\n     *\n     * <p>The iteration count is read from the credential file, so a record can order this class to do\n     * eighty times the work every other account costs - eighty times the CPU on the machine, and\n     * eighty times the delay before the latency floor releases. Two findings share that one number:\n     * the delay is an account-existence oracle that survives the uniform-work design (the equalising\n     * floor is measured against the configured cost, so a record that costs more than the floor is\n     * simply late), and it is a cheap denial of service against the four-permit work budget - a\n     * handful of such records holds every login slot. A record cannot legitimately ask for more work\n     * than the deployment's own current policy, so this class refuses it rather than performing it.\n     */\n    private final int maxSaltIterations;\n    private final SecureRandom random;\n\n    PasswordHasher(char[] pepperChars, int iterations, SecureRandom random) throws AuthException {\n        if (pepperChars == null || pepperChars.length < MIN_PEPPER_BYTES || iterations < 1) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        if (iterations > MAX_ITERATIONS) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        byte[] encoded = InputValidator.encodePassword(pepperChars);\n        if (encoded.length < MIN_PEPPER_BYTES) {\n            Arrays.fill(encoded, (byte) 0);\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        this.pepper = encoded;\n        this.iterations = iterations;\n        this.maxSaltIterations = iterations;\n        this.random = random;\n    }\n\n    byte[] newSalt() {\n        byte[] salt = new byte[SALT_BYTES];\n        random.nextBytes(salt);\n        return salt;\n    }\n\n    int iterations() {\n        return iterations;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Derives the digest to store.\n     *\n     * @param passwordUtf8 UTF-8 bytes of the password, already validated for exact encodability\n     */\n    byte[] derive(byte[] passwordUtf8, byte[] salt, int saltIterations) throws AuthException {\n        if (salt == null || salt.length != SALT_BYTES) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        if (saltIterations < 1 || saltIterations > maxSaltIterations) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        if (passwordUtf8 == null || passwordUtf8.length == 0) {\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.PASSWORD_TOO_SHORT);\n        }\n        // PBEKeySpec takes characters, so the validated UTF-8 bytes are turned back into the\n        // ISO-8859-1 view of the same bytes. This is a bijection, so it cannot merge two distinct\n        // passwords - unlike the platform default charset, which folds unrepresentable characters.\n        char[] asChars = iso88591Chars(passwordUtf8);\n        PBEKeySpec spec = new PBEKeySpec(asChars, salt, saltIterations, DERIVED_KEY_BITS);\n        Arrays.fill(asChars, (char) 0);\n        final byte[] derived;\n        try {\n            SecretKeyFactory factory = SecretKeyFactory.getInstance(KDF_ALGORITHM);\n            derived = factory.generateSecret(spec).getEncoded();\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException | RuntimeException e) {\n            spec.clearPassword();\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        spec.clearPassword();\n        try {\n            Mac mac = Mac.getInstance(MAC_ALGORITHM);\n            mac.init(new SecretKeySpec(pepper, MAC_ALGORITHM));\n            mac.update(salt);\n            byte[] digest = mac.doFinal(derived);\n            Arrays.fill(derived, (byte) 0);\n            return digest;\n        } catch (GeneralSecurityException | RuntimeException e) {\n            Arrays.fill(derived, (byte) 0);\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Checks a candidate password against a stored digest in constant time with respect to the\n     * digest contents.\n     *\n     * @return {@code true} only when the candidate reproduces the stored digest\n     */\n    boolean matches(byte[] passwordUtf8, byte[] salt, int saltIterations, byte[] storedDigest) throws AuthException {\n        if (storedDigest == null || storedDigest.length != MAX_DIGEST_BYTES) {\n            // A truncated or oversized stored digest cannot be checked; treat as a store problem\n            // so the attempt fails closed instead of comparing against a padded value.\n            throw new AuthException(Diagnostic.STORE_UNAVAILABLE);\n        }\n        byte[] candidate = derive(passwordUtf8, salt, saltIterations);\n        try {\n            return MessageDigest.isEqual(candidate, storedDigest);\n        } finally {\n            Arrays.fill(candidate, (byte) 0);\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Decodes a Base64 field from the credential file, requiring the exact canonical form so that\n     * two different textual values can never denote the same salt.\n     *\n     * <p>There is deliberately no matching encoder. This component reads credential records and\n     * never writes them: rendering a record is provisioning, which is account creation, and outside\n     * the scope of an authentication component.\n     *\n     * @return the decoded bytes, or {@code null} when the text is not canonical Base64\n     */\n    static byte[] decodeBytes(String value, int expectedLength) {\n        if (value == null || value.isEmpty()) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        final byte[] decoded;\n        try {\n            decoded = Base64.getDecoder().decode(value);\n        } catch (IllegalArgumentException e) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (decoded.length != expectedLength) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (!Base64.getEncoder().encodeToString(decoded).equals(value)) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        return decoded;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Measures how long {@code iterations} of PBKDF2 take on this machine, used by the latency\n     * budget in {@link CredentialVerifier} so the budget tracks the configured work factor instead\n     * of a hard-coded guess.\n     */\n    long measureDerivationNanos(int iterations, byte[] probePassword) {\n        byte[] salt = newSalt();\n        long best = Long.MAX_VALUE;\n        for (int i = 0; i < 2; i++) {\n            long start = System.nanoTime();\n            try {\n                derive(probePassword, salt, iterations);\n            } catch (AuthException e) {\n                return -1L;\n            }\n            long elapsed = System.nanoTime() - start;\n            if (elapsed < best) {\n                best = elapsed;\n            }\n        }\n        return best;\n    }\n\n    void erase() {\n        Arrays.fill(pepper, (byte) 0);\n    }\n\n    private static char[] iso88591Chars(byte[] bytes) {\n        char[] out = new char[bytes.length];\n        for (int i = 0; i < bytes.length; i++) {\n            out[i] = (char) (bytes[i] & 0xff);\n        }\n        return out;\n    }\n\n    static byte[] utf8(String value) {\n        return value.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"user","uuid":"f49855ff-967b-42c5-bb7d-0d29059c3c18","session_id":"31967372-02de-4ae0-b5e8-f3ae53c5d120","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_7518f27af5094e39bdb6950e","is_error":false,"content":"package com.example.auth;\n\nimport java.io.IOException;\nimport java.nio.charset.StandardCharsets;\nimport java.nio.file.Files;\nimport java.nio.file.LinkOption;\nimport java.nio.file.Path;\nimport java.util.ArrayList;\nimport java.util.Collections;\nimport java.util.LinkedHashMap;\nimport java.util.List;\nimport java.util.Map;\nimport java.util.Optional;\n\n/**\n * Credential records loaded from the configured file.\n *\n * <p>The file is operator-supplied and therefore attacker-controlled: anyone with write access to it\n * can try to shape a security outcome, so every field is validated on load and any problem is fatal\n * at start-up rather than tolerated. No URL, ZIP or remote URI scheme is accepted anywhere; the path\n * must be an absolute local path, checked for {@code NOFOLLOW_LINKS} regular-file status before it\n * is read.\n *\n * <p>Record format, one record per line, UTF-8:\n * <pre>username : base64(16-byte salt) : iteration-count : base64(32-byte digest)</pre>\n * A valid username cannot contain {@code :}, so the split is unambiguous. Rejected outright, each\n * with a start-up problem that names only the line number and never the offending content:\n * <ul>\n *   <li>a username that fails {@link InputValidator};</li>\n *   <li>Base64 that is not canonical, or of the wrong length;</li>\n *   <li>an iteration count below the configured minimum or above the hard maximum;</li>\n *   <li>duplicate usernames, and usernames that collide with another record ignoring ASCII case -\n *       either would let one spelling name two different records, so the store refuses to load;</li>\n *   <li>more records than {@link #MAX_RECORDS}, so the file cannot be used to exhaust memory.</li>\n * </ul>\n */\nfinal class FileCredentialStore implements CredentialStore {\n\n    static final int MAX_RECORDS = 20_000;\n    private static final int MAX_FILE_BYTES = 8 * 1024 * 1024;\n    private static final int MAX_LINE_CHARS = 512;\n\n    private final Map<String, AccountCredentials> byExactName;\n    private final Map<String, String> canonicalByLowerName;\n\n    private FileCredentialStore(Map<String, AccountCredentials> byExactName, Map<String, String> canonicalByLowerName) {\n        this.byExactName = Collections.unmodifiableMap(byExactName);\n        this.canonicalByLowerName = Collections.unmodifiableMap(canonicalByLowerName);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Loads and validates the credential file.\n     *\n     * @param problems start-up problems are appended here; nothing is thrown so that the caller can\n     *     report every problem found across configuration and store together\n     */\n    static FileCredentialStore load(Path file, AuthConfig config, List<String> problems) {\n        Map<String, AccountCredentials> byExactName = new LinkedHashMap<>();\n        Map<String, String> canonicalByLowerName = new LinkedHashMap<>();\n        if (file == null) {\n            problems.add(\"credential file is missing\");\n            return new FileCredentialStore(byExactName, canonicalByLowerName);\n        }\n        if (!Files.isRegularFile(file, LinkOption.NOFOLLOW_LINKS)) {\n            problems.add(\"credential file is not a regular file\");\n            return new FileCredentialStore(byExactName, canonicalByLowerName);\n        }\n        final long size;\n        try {\n            size = Files.size(file);\n        } catch (IOException e) {\n            problems.add(\"credential file size could not be determined\");\n            return new FileCredentialStore(byExactName, canonicalByLowerName);\n        }\n        if (size > MAX_FILE_BYTES) {\n            problems.add(\"credential file is larger than the accepted size\");\n            return new FileCredentialStore(byExactName, canonicalByLowerName);\n        }\n        final List<String> lines;\n        try {\n            lines = Files.readAllLines(file, StandardCharsets.UTF_8);\n        } catch (IOException | RuntimeException e) {\n            problems.add(\"credential file could not be read as UTF-8 text\");\n            return new FileCredentialStore(byExactName, canonicalByLowerName);\n        }\n        if (lines.size() > MAX_RECORDS) {\n            problems.add(\"credential file has more records than the accepted maximum\");\n            return new FileCredentialStore(byExactName, canonicalByLowerName);\n        }\n\n        int lineNumber = 0;\n        for (String line : lines) {\n            lineNumber++;\n            if (line.isEmpty() || line.startsWith(\"#\")) {\n                continue;\n            }\n            if (line.length() > MAX_LINE_CHARS) {\n                problems.add(\"credential file line \" + lineNumber + \" is too long\");\n                continue;\n            }\n            AccountCredentials record = parseLine(line, lineNumber, config, problems);\n            if (record == null) {\n                continue;\n            }\n            String exact = record.username();\n            if (byExactName.containsKey(exact)) {\n                problems.add(\"credential file line \" + lineNumber + \" repeats a username\");\n                continue;\n            }\n            String lower = lowerAscii(exact);\n            if (canonicalByLowerName.containsKey(lower)) {\n                problems.add(\"credential file line \" + lineNumber + \" collides with an earlier username ignoring case\");\n                continue;\n            }\n            byExactName.put(exact, record);\n            canonicalByLowerName.put(lower, exact);\n        }\n        if (!problems.isEmpty()) {\n            // Fail closed: a store we cannot fully trust is not loaded at all. The caller refuses\n            // to start the service once the collected problems are reported.\n            return new FileCredentialStore(new LinkedHashMap<>(), new LinkedHashMap<>());\n        }\n        return new FileCredentialStore(byExactName, canonicalByLowerName);\n    }\n\n    private static AccountCredentials parseLine(String line, int lineNumber, AuthConfig config, List<String> problems) {\n        String[] parts = line.split(\":\", -1);\n        if (parts.length != 4) {\n            problems.add(\"credential file line \" + lineNumber + \" does not have four fields\");\n            return null;\n        }\n        String username = InputValidator.validateUsername(parts[0]);\n        if (username == null) {\n            problems.add(\"credential file line \" + lineNumber + \" has an invalid username\");\n            return null;\n        }\n        byte[] salt = PasswordHasher.decodeBytes(parts[1], PasswordHasher.SALT_BYTES);\n        if (salt == null) {\n            problems.add(\"credential file line \" + lineNumber + \" has an invalid salt\");\n            return null;\n        }\n        int iterations = parseIterations(parts[2]);\n        // The upper bound is the configured count, not the parser's ceiling. A record may not order\n        // this deployment to do more work per sign-in than its own policy costs every other account:\n        // PasswordHasher enforces the same bound at derivation time, and refusing here turns it into\n        // a named start-up problem instead of a per-attempt store failure.\n        if (iterations < config.minAcceptedIterations() || iterations > config.pbkdf2Iterations()) {\n            problems.add(\"credential file line \" + lineNumber + \" has an unacceptable iteration count\");\n            return null;\n        }\n        byte[] digest = PasswordHasher.decodeBytes(parts[3], PasswordHasher.MAX_DIGEST_BYTES);\n        if (digest == null) {\n            problems.add(\"credential file line \" + lineNumber + \" has an invalid digest\");\n            return null;\n        }\n        return new AccountCredentials(username, salt, iterations, digest);\n    }\n\n    private static int parseIterations(String field) {\n        if (field.isEmpty() || field.length() > 9) {\n            return -1;\n        }\n        for (int i = 0; i < field.length(); i++) {\n            if (field.charAt(i) < '0' || field.charAt(i) > '9') {\n                return -1;\n            }\n        }\n        if (field.length() > 1 && field.charAt(0) == '0') {\n            return -1;\n        }\n        try {\n            return Integer.parseInt(field);\n        } catch (NumberFormatException e) {\n            return -1;\n        }\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    public Optional<AccountCredentials> lookup(String username) {\n        if (username == null) {\n            return Optional.empty();\n        }\n        String canonical = resolve(username);\n        if (canonical == null) {\n            return Optional.empty();\n        }\n        AccountCredentials record = byExactName.get(canonical);\n        return record == null ? Optional.empty() : Optional.of(record);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Canonical name that {@code username} names, or {@code null}. ASCII case is the only thing\n     * folded, and load-time validation guarantees at most one record per folded name.\n     */\n    String resolve(String username) {\n        if (username == null) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        return canonicalByLowerName.get(lowerAscii(username));\n    }\n\n    @Override\n    public int size() {\n        return byExactName.size();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * ASCII-only case folding. It is used solely to detect two records whose names differ only in\n     * case and to let one record be named by either spelling. The stored name is never rewritten, so\n     * this cannot merge two records and cannot make a non-ASCII look-alike resolve to an account.\n     */\n    private static String lowerAscii(String value) {\n        StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder(value.length());\n        for (int i = 0; i < value.length(); i++) {\n            char c = value.charAt(i);\n            if (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') {\n                c = (char) (c + 32);\n            }\n            out.append(c);\n        }\n        return out.toString();\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"918b689c-8a7e-4256-abc7-6f5711073818","session_id":"31967372-02de-4ae0-b5e8-f3ae53c5d120","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"918b689c-8a7e-4256-abc7-6f5711073818","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Both PasswordHasher and FileCredentialStore look solid. com.example.auth;\n\nimport java.security.SecureRandom;\nimport java.util.Iterator;\nimport java.util.LinkedHashMap;\nimport java.util.Map;\n\n/**\n * Issues and validates session identifiers.\n *\n * <p>Identifiers are 32 characters of lower-case hexadecimal - 128 bits from\n * {@link SecureRandom#getStrongInstance()} - generated by this code at the moment a sign-in is\n * verified. They are never accepted from a caller as an input to sign-in, never derived from\n * anything the caller supplies, and never predictable: there is no counter, timestamp, MAC address\n * or hash-of-the-account involved.\n *\n * <p>Validity is decided only by presence in this table, which is server-side state. A\n * well-formed-but-unknown identifier is simply not valid; there is no self-contained token to trust\n * and nothing to parse, so a caller cannot construct one.\n *\n * <p><b>Atomicity.</b> Issue, validate-and-touch, revoke and capacity bookkeeping all happen under\n * one lock on the map. Expired entries are dropped inside the same critical section as a validation,\n * so an expired session cannot be validated by one thread while another is still touching it.\n *\n * <p><b>Bounded.</b> At most {@code maxActiveSessions} identifiers exist; when the table is full the\n * oldest-expiring entry is evicted and a fresh issue is refused only if no entry can be reclaimed.\n * Refusing to issue (rather than dropping capacity limits) is what keeps memory bounded under a\n * script that signs in continuously.\n *\n * <p><b>Residual risk, documented in the README:</b> the table is in-process. Behind more than one\n * node, a session issued by one node is unknown to another; a deployment needs a shared session\n * store. In-memory identifiers are also not protected at rest - they live in the heap, which is why\n * the README lists heap dumping as a residual risk and why the transport requirements below apply.\n *\n * <p><b>Cookie contract for the caller.</b> This component returns the identifier only; the caller\n * must set it as a cookie with {@code HttpOnly}, {@code Secure}, {@code SameSite=Strict}, a\n * {@code Path} no broader than the application root, and an {@code Expires}/{@code Max-Age} no\n * longer than {@link #ttlSeconds()}. It must not place the identifier in a URL, in a response body,\n * in a log line, or in a {@code Referrer-Policy}-permitted link. A helper that builds the\n * {@code Set-Cookie} value is provided in {@link #cookieDirectiveFor(String, String, String, boolean)}\n * so the flags cannot be forgotten by an integrator; it emits {@code HttpOnly}, {@code Secure} and\n * {@code SameSite=Strict} on a TLS request and returns {@code null} for a plaintext one, so a caller\n * that cannot say whether the connection is TLS gets no cookie at all rather than one that could\n * travel in clear text. The four-argument form is the only one an integrator should reach for: the\n * three-argument overload is a convenience for code that has already proven it is on TLS, and it\n * asserts {@code secureTransport = true} on the caller's behalf.\n */\nfinal class SessionTokenStore {\n\n    /** Hex characters: 32 of them encode 128 bits. */\n    static final int TOKEN_HEX_LENGTH = 32;\n\n    private static final long MIN_EVICTION_GRACE_MILLIS = 1_000L;\n\n    private final SecureRandom random;\n    private final int ttlSeconds;\n    private final int maxActiveSessions;\n    private final TimeSource time;\n    private final LinkedHashMap<String, Long> expiresAtMillis = new LinkedHashMap<>();\n\n    private long issuedCount;\n    private long evictedCount;\n\n    SessionTokenStore(SecureRandom random, int ttlSeconds, int maxActiveSessions, TimeSource time) {\n        this.random = random;\n        this.ttlSeconds = ttlSeconds;\n        this.maxActiveSessions = maxActiveSessions;\n        this.time = time;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Issues a new identifier.\n     *\n     * @return the identifier, or {@code null} when capacity is exhausted and nothing could be\n     *     reclaimed. The caller must then fail the sign-in: issuing beyond capacity is not an\n     *     option, and neither is silently evicting a live user's session to make room.\n     */\n    synchronized String issue() {\n        long now = time.nowMillis();\n        dropExpiredLocked(now);\n        if (expiresAtMillis.size() >= maxActiveSessions) {\n            if (!evictOldestExpiringLocked(now)) {\n                return null;\n            }\n            evictedCount++;\n        }\n        String token = newToken();\n        expiresAtMillis.put(token, now + ttlSeconds * 1000L);\n        issuedCount++;\n        return token;\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Validates an identifier and slides its expiry on success. Validity is presence in this table\n     * and nothing else.\n     *\n     * <p>The shape check runs <em>first</em>, outside the lock. Everything a caller can send arrives\n     * unauthenticated, and a table scan on the far side of that check would let anyone holding a\n     * keyboard convert the expiry sweep into a cost that scales with the deployment's session cap\n     * (100&nbsp;000 entries) rather than with the traffic they can actually generate. The sweep is\n     * therefore reached only by a string that is already the right shape - i.e. one that is far more\n     * likely to be a real identifier than an attacker's first probe.\n     */\n    synchronized boolean isValid(String candidate) {\n        if (candidate == null || candidate.length() != TOKEN_HEX_LENGTH || !isHex(candidate)) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        long now = time.nowMillis();\n        dropExpiredLocked(now);\n        // The lookup is a hash probe: no byte-by-byte comparison against a stored identifier ever\n        // happens, so there is nothing for a timing measurement to follow. Guessing is ruled out by\n        // the 128-bit space, not by comparison timing.\n        Long expiry = expiresAtMillis.get(candidate);\n        if (expiry == null || expiry <= now) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        expiresAtMillis.put(candidate, now + ttlSeconds * 1000L);\n        return true;\n    }\n\n    /** Revokes an identifier. Idempotent; unknown identifiers are ignored. */\n    synchronized void revoke(String candidate) {\n        if (candidate == null || candidate.length() != TOKEN_HEX_LENGTH) {\n            return;\n        }\n        expiresAtMillis.remove(candidate);\n    }\n\n    /** Number of live identifiers, after dropping expired ones. */\n    synchronized int activeCount() {\n        dropExpiredLocked(time.nowMillis());\n        return expiresAtMillis.size();\n    }\n\n    int ttlSeconds() {\n        return ttlSeconds;\n    }\n\n    int maxActiveSessions() {\n        return maxActiveSessions;\n    }\n\n    synchronized long issuedCount() {\n        return issuedCount;\n    }\n\n    synchronized long evictedCount() {\n        return evictedCount;\n    }\n\n    synchronized void clear() {\n        expiresAtMillis.clear();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Convenience form for a caller that has already established the request is on TLS.\n     *\n     * <p>It asserts {@code secureTransport = true}, so on a plaintext request the browser discards\n     * the cookie rather than the session travelling in clear text - the failure is a lost sign-in, not\n     * an exposure. Integrators who cannot prove the transport should call the four-argument form and\n     * pass what they actually know.\n     *\n     * @param candidate the identifier returned by {@link AuthResult#sessionId()}\n     * @param cookieName must be a bare token: {@code [A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}}\n     * @param path must be an absolute path of at most 128 characters, no control characters, no\n     *     comma, semicolon or CR/LF\n     * @return the directive, or {@code null} when any input is unusable\n     */\n    public String cookieDirectiveFor(String candidate, String cookieName, String path) {\n        return cookieDirectiveFor(candidate, cookieName, path, true);\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Builds the {@code Set-Cookie} value for a session identifier, with the flags an integrator is\n     * most likely to forget.\n     *\n     * <p>{@code Secure} is included only when {@code secureTransport} is {@code true}. A caller that\n     * cannot say whether the request arrived over TLS gets no directive at all rather than a\n     * cookie that could travel in clear text; that is the fail-closed choice.\n     *\n     * @param candidate the identifier returned by {@link AuthResult#sessionId()}\n     * @param cookieName must be a bare token: {@code [A-Za-z0-9_-]{1,64}}\n     * @param path must be an absolute path of at most 128 characters, no control characters, no\n     *     comma, semicolon or CR/LF\n     * @param secureTransport whether the request arrived over TLS; {@code false} yields {@code null}\n     * @return the directive, or {@code null} when any input is unusable or the transport is not TLS\n     */\n    public String cookieDirectiveFor(String candidate, String cookieName, String path, boolean secureTransport) {\n        if (!secureTransport) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (candidate == null || !isHex(candidate)) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (candidate.length() != TOKEN_HEX_LENGTH) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (!isCookieName(cookieName)) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        if (!isCookiePath(path)) {\n            return null;\n        }\n        return cookieName + \"=\" + candidate\n                + \"; Path=\" + path\n                + \"; Max-Age=\" + ttlSeconds\n                + \"; HttpOnly; SameSite=Strict; Secure\";\n    }\n\n    private static boolean isCookieName(String name) {\n        if (name == null || name.isEmpty() || name.length() > 64) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        for (int i = 0; i < name.length(); i++) {\n            char c = name.charAt(i);\n            boolean allowed = (c >= 'a' && c <= 'z') || (c >= 'A' && c <= 'Z') || (c >= '0' && c <= '9')\n                    || c == '_' || c == '-';\n            if (!allowed) {\n                return false;\n            }\n        }\n        return true;\n    }\n\n    private static boolean isCookiePath(String path) {\n        if (path == null || path.length() < 1 || path.length() > 128 || path.charAt(0) != '/') {\n            return false;\n        }\n        for (int i = 0; i < path.length(); i++) {\n            char c = path.charAt(i);\n            if (c <= 0x20 || c == 0x7f || c == ';' || c == ',' || c == '\"' || c == '\\\\') {\n                return false;\n            }\n        }\n        return true;\n    }\n\n    private static boolean isHex(String value) {\n        for (int i = 0; i < value.length(); i++) {\n            char c = value.charAt(i);\n            if (!((c >= '0' && c <= '9') || (c >= 'a' && c <= 'f'))) {\n                return false;\n            }\n        }\n        return true;\n    }\n\n    private String newToken() {\n        byte[] raw = new byte[TOKEN_HEX_LENGTH / 2];\n        random.nextBytes(raw);\n        StringBuilder out = new StringBuilder(TOKEN_HEX_LENGTH);\n        for (byte b : raw) {\n            out.append(Character.forDigit((b >> 4) & 0xf, 16));\n            out.append(Character.forDigit(b & 0xf, 16));\n        }\n        return out.toString();\n    }\n\n    private void dropExpiredLocked(long now) {\n        Iterator<Map.Entry<String, Long>> it = expiresAtMillis.entrySet().iterator();\n        while (it.hasNext()) {\n            if (it.next().getValue() <= now) {\n                it.remove();\n            }\n        }\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Reclaims one entry when the table is full, choosing the identifier that expires soonest.\n     *\n     * <p>Only entries that are at least {@value #MIN_EVICTION_GRACE_MILLIS} old are eligible, so a\n     * session that was just issued to a real user is not dropped by the next sign-in.\n     */\n    private boolean evictOldestExpiringLocked(long now) {\n        String victim = null;\n        long best = Long.MAX_VALUE;\n        long ttlMillis = ttlSeconds * 1000L;\n        for (Map.Entry<String, Long> entry : expiresAtMillis.entrySet()) {\n            long expiry = entry.getValue();\n            // Age, not expiry: an entry issued ttlMillis before its expiry was issued at\n            // \"expiry - ttlMillis\". Deriving this wrongly makes every entry look ancient, which\n            // turns the capacity limit into \"disconnect whoever signed in earliest\" instead of\n            // \"refuse a new session\" - so saturating the table would log users out on demand.\n            long ageMillis = now - (expiry - ttlMillis);\n            if (ageMillis < MIN_EVICTION_GRACE_MILLIS) {\n                continue;\n            }\n            if (expiry < best) {\n                best = expiry;\n                victim = entry.getKey();\n            }\n        }\n        if (victim == null) {\n            return false;\n        }\n        expiresAtMillis.remove(victim);\n        return true;\n    }\n\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"user","uuid":"9a836e37-34cf-40a7-bcd3-711e30a69508","session_id":"31967372-02de-4ae0-b5e8-f3ae53c5d120","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"role":"user","content":[{"type":"tool_result","tool_use_id":"call_2c051e7777b8432a91887fb0","is_error":false,"content":"package com.example.auth;\n\n/**\n * One credential record as held in the store.\n *\n * <p>Immutable, and it deliberately holds no password: only the salt, the iteration count that was\n * used, and the resulting digest. The digest is treated as secret - it is never logged and never\n * returned to a caller of the service.\n */\nfinal class AccountCredentials {\n\n    private final String username;\n    private final byte[] salt;\n    private final int iterations;\n    private final byte[] digest;\n\n    AccountCredentials(String username, byte[] salt, int iterations, byte[] digest) {\n        this.username = username;\n        this.salt = salt;\n        this.iterations = iterations;\n        this.digest = digest;\n    }\n\n    String username() {\n        return username;\n    }\n\n    byte[] salt() {\n        return salt.clone();\n    }\n\n    int iterations() {\n        return iterations;\n    }\n\n    byte[] digest() {\n        return digest.clone();\n    }\n\n    /**\n     * Deliberately no serialiser. Rendering a credential line is provisioning, which is account\n     * creation, and this component is scoped to authentication and event logging: a shipped writer\n     * would be a production path that turns a record back into a file, and one more place where a\n     * digest could be written somewhere its owner did not approve. The test tree builds its own\n     * lines. {@link #toString()} likewise omits both secret fields, so an accidental log or exception\n     * message containing a record cannot publish a crackable verifier.\n     */\n    @Override\n    public String toString() {\n        return \"AccountCredentials[\" + username + \", iterations=\" + iterations + \"]\";\n    }\n}\n"}]}},{"type":"assistant","uuid":"386439fa-52cf-4702-9b17-59927aebabea","session_id":"31967372-02de-4ae0-b5e8-f3ae53c5d120","parent_tool_use_id":null,"message":{"id":"386439fa-52cf-4702-9b17-59927aebabea","type":"message","role":"assistant","model":"vllm-general","content":[{"type":"thinking","thinking":"Confirmed the javadoc of SessionTokenStore: \"128 bits worth from {@link SecureRandom#getStrongInstance()}\" — this is a claim, but the Builder injects `new SecureRandom()` along with a documented rationale. The javadoc's claim is wrong / stale. Security impact: low (the default SecureRandom is a CSPRNG). This is a finding regarding a mismatch between the claim and the implementation — informational / low severity, CWE-1059 (insufficient documentation)? Hmm, CWE-1059's name is \"Insufficient Technical Documentation\". Fits as low.\n\nOK, remaining questions regarding the seeded flaw. My best candidate at this point:\n\nCandidate A (medium): evictIdleSources removes blocked source buckets → early release of a throttling block under table pressure (shortening the block period down to the window's idle time). CWE-307. Re-check the actual math:\n\n- consumeSourceAttempt: when a new key arrives and the table is full: `if (sources.size() >= MAX_SOURCE_KEYS && !evictIdleSources(horizon))`, where horizon = nowMillis - windowSeconds*1000L.\n- evictIdleSources(horizon) deletes all buckets with lastTouchedMillis < horizon — including buckets where blockedUntilMillis > now.\n- Blocked source S: on each attempt lastTouched is updated (assignment comes first). If S stops attempting for more than windowSeconds and some other new source key triggers eviction, S's bucket is deleted despite still being blocked (when blockSeconds > windowSeconds, it's possible that blockedUntil is still in the future).\n- When S comes back: new bucket, full budget — the remainder of the block is erased.\n- Precondition: table is full (20,000 keys). The attacker can fill it: consumeSourceAttempt creates a bucket on the first attempt from each distinct source address. 20,000 distinct IPs — reachable for a botnet-scale attacker, and this is the very fail-closed scenario the class claims to handle. Ironically, the blockedForever() path handles the case where the table is full and nothing can be evicted — but the buggy evictIdleSources always finds something to evict as long as blocked-but-idle buckets exist.\n\nWait — even without blocked buckets, as long as any bucket is older than windowSeconds, eviction will find it. The question is whether eviction deletes blocked buckets. It does. The guard in sweepIfDue shows the intended invariant: only sweep when `blockedUntilMillis <= nowMillis`. evictIdleSources lacks this. Severity medium.\n\nevictIdleAccounts similarly: horizon = nowMillis - lockSeconds*2000L — the 2x margin guarantees the lock has expired (the lock lasts for lockSeconds from the last update; the update happens on the tripping failure and each subsequent recordAccountFailure… wait, but during the lock, does recordAccountFailure get called for that account? No: authenticate checks checkAccountLock first; if LOCKED it returns before the credential check, so recordAccountFailure is never reached during the lock. checkAccountLock does not update lastTouchedMillis. So the lastTouched of a locked account = time of lock activation (or last failure). Lock ends at lastTouched + lockSeconds. Eviction requires lastTouched < now - 2*lockSeconds → now > lastTouched + 2*lockSeconds > lock end time + lockSeconds. So the account bucket is only evicted after the lock expires, plus a full lockSeconds of margin. Account side safe. Source side: no such margin — horizon = windowSeconds, and block is blockSeconds, which may be larger than window. Asymmetric.\n\nHmm, wait: but is it also guaranteed on the source side that the block can outlive the window? AuthConfig bounds: need to check the ranges of sourceWindowSeconds and sourceBlockSeconds. From the summary: MAX_WINDOW_SECONDS exists (=3600?). blockSeconds is probably 1..3600 too. So block > window is allowed (e.g., window 60s / max 5 attempts, block 3600s — typical). Yes, a real inconsistency.\n\nCandidate B (low): the `lastTouchedMillis = System.currentTimeMillis()` field initializer contradicts the javadoc's single-clock invariant of sweepIfDue; currently harmless because every creation site stamps immediately. Potential defect. CWE-1188 (unsafe default initialization of a resource). Low / informational.\n\nHmm, actually wait — is there a creation path that doesn't stamp? Let me recheck consumeSourceAttempt: bucket == null → possibly create → then `synchronized (bucket) { bucket.lastTouchedMillis = nowMillis; ... }` — yes, it stamps. recordAccountFailure: same. So harmless. Report as low (potential).\n\nCandidate C (low): SessionTokenStore javadoc vs `new SecureRandom()`. CWE-1059 low.\n\nCandidate D (low / suggestion): minAcceptedIterations floor 10,000 / default 50,000 < policy floor 120,000, no upgrade-on-login rehash (by design, no write path).
